<agent-message from="research-android">
Recommendation: build a native Kotlin app with Jetpack Compose Material 3 on phone, Compose for Wear only if/when a watch APK is actually needed, and optional Horologist for Data Layer helpers. For v1, use PHONE-GENERATED BRIDGED NOTIFICATIONS, not a Wear app. Use FCM high-priority data messages for red wakeups; use REST/WebSocket over always-on Tailscale only for foreground dashboard/transcript streaming and reply sync. Self-hosted ntfy+UnifiedPush is the fallback, not a permanent app-owned WebSocket.

MATRIX
- Native Kotlin + phone Compose + Wear Compose: watch 5/5, foldable 5/5, Android integrations 5/5, solo risk lowest. Official docs call Compose for Wear the recommended UI approach, and Wear Material 3 is current: https://developer.android.com/training/wearables/compose . Use Horologist selectively; it is Google-maintained gap-filling tooling, but main is a moving 0.8.x line while 0.7.x is maintenance: https://github.com/google/horologist
- Kotlin Multiplatform: shared domain/network code is possible, but there is no distinct first-class Wear OS UI target; Wear UI still uses Android-only androidx.wear.compose. With no iOS target, KMP adds build/source-set complexity for little benefit. Compose MP officially lists Android/iOS/desktop/web, not Wear OS: https://kotlinlang.org/docs/multiplatform/supported-platforms.html
- Flutter: generic Android is supported but Wear OS is not a first-class listed/CI target and there is no official Wear component stack; notification/Data Layer/watch packaging require native glue. https://docs.flutter.dev/reference/supported-platforms
- React Native/Expo: weakest watch choice. RN officially lists Android/iOS in-tree and several out-of-tree platforms, but no Wear OS; Expo’s platform docs likewise do not list Wear OS. https://reactnative.dev/docs/out-of-tree-platforms and https://docs.expo.dev/versions/latest/

WATCH MODEL
1) Bridged phone notification (recommended v1): Android says phone notifications bridge to paired watches by default, specifically calling this ideal when no wearable app is installed. Expanded notifications provide a scrollable content/actions view. Wear docs explicitly support voice replies and predefined responses via RemoteInput.setChoices(): https://developer.android.com/training/wearables/notifications . Build one NotificationCompat reply action with 2–4 setChoices values AND leave setAllowFreeFormInput(true) (default), so the same action offers canned taps plus watch voice/keyboard free text. AOSP confirms choices+free-form can coexist: https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/app/RemoteInput.java . Do not create one action per answer: Android’s current generic notification limit is 3 actions; one RemoteInput avoids that: https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/app/Notification.java . Use NotificationCompat styles, not RemoteViews (custom layouts are stripped on Wear). If WearableExtender.addAction() is used, it replaces normal builder actions on watch, so do not use it for this basic flow.
2) Important hard blocker: “full question text” is not unbounded in a system notification. Current AOSP clamps each Notification Builder CharSequence to 1,024 UTF-16 code units; FCM total payload is 4,096 bytes. Source for 1,024 clamp: Notification.java above (`MAX_CHARSEQUENCE_LENGTH`, `safeCharSequence`); FCM limit: https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/customize-messages/set-message-type . Therefore pure bridging is sufficient only if the event API validates a <=1,024-character watch question contract. If arbitrary-length full text is non-negotiable, a pure notification cannot guarantee it: ship a minimal Wear companion whose red notification opens a Compose question screen containing full text/options. That is the only material reason this requirement may force a watch UI.
3) A Data Layer watch app is technically NON-STANDALONE: it still depends on the paired phone. Data Layer automatically chooses encrypted Bluetooth or E2E-encrypted Google cloud relay when BT is unavailable, and requires identical package name/signature: https://developer.android.com/training/wearables/data/overview . A true standalone watch app needs its own FCM/network path and an internet-accessible reply relay. Android’s definition: https://developer.android.com/training/wearables/apps/standalone-apps .
4) Phone-away behavior: Data Layer can cloud-route over watch LTE/Wi-Fi, but phone dependence remains. Pixel Watch support says synced texts require the connected phone to be online: https://support.google.com/googlepixelwatch/answer/12674814 . Treat bridged notifications similarly: LTE/Wi-Fi watch + online phone should work, but it is not independence from a dead/offline phone. Pixel Watch 4 live-device testing is still required for exact bridged RemoteInput presentation.
5) Standalone Wear app is needed only for: arbitrary text beyond notification clamp/custom screen, guaranteed operation when phone is offline/dead, watch-local history/state, or custom multi-step UI. It is not needed merely for 2–4 choices + free text.

PUSH/TRANSPORT
- Primary FCM: supported for sideloaded apps. Firebase explicitly says apps are not limited to Play Store deployment; device needs Google Play services/Play Store, which Pixel phone/watch have. Also request Android 13+ POST_NOTIFICATIONS. https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/android/client
- Send HIGH-priority DATA-only messages only for red/user-visible events; decrypt/validate, persist, then immediately post the rich local NotificationCompat notification. High priority can wake Doze, grants a short processing/network window, and can be deprioritized after 7 days of high messages that do not produce visible notifications. Extra work should use expedited WorkManager. https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/android/message-priority
- FCM is not E2E; encrypt sensitive question/options in the data payload or send opaque ID. Keep the Firebase service-account credential only on desktop. Use event/reply idempotency IDs. If >4 KB, send ID and fetch from desktop, but notification still cannot show >1,024 in one CharSequence.
- Tailscale transport: use a live WebSocket while phone UI is open and REST for transcript pages/reply outbox. Do not use an app-owned background WebSocket as urgent push. Doze suspends network/wakelocks/jobs; FCM gets a temporary exception, and Android warns multiple per-app persistent sockets drain battery: https://developer.android.com/training/monitoring-device-state/doze-standby . A foreground service plus battery exemption can make it work, but adds permanent notification/battery cost and still is a poor primary.
- Fallback ntfy: direct ntfy Android notifications support only up to 3 fixed actions and no RemoteInput/free-text action, so they cannot directly satisfy 4 choices + free text: https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/#action-buttons . Instead integrate the UnifiedPush connector in OUR app and use the ntfy app as distributor; it delivers raw 1–4,096-byte payloads, then our app posts its own RemoteInput notification: https://unifiedpush.org/developers/spec/android/ and https://unifiedpush.org/kdoc/connector/ . Self-hosted ntfy uses WebSocket/foreground-service instant delivery rather than FCM and needs battery exemption/permanent notification: https://docs.ntfy.sh/subscribe/phone/ . This is viable redundancy, but heavier than FCM.

FOLDABLE UX
Use `NavigableListDetailPaneScaffold`: expanded windows show workspace/agent list + transcript side-by-side; compact/smaller windows show one pane and navigate list→detail. It includes navigator/back handling and predictive-back animation. https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/layouts/adaptive/list-detail . Drive it from `currentWindowAdaptiveInfo()` and window size/posture, never model/orientation checks; window size can change in split-screen. Persist selected conversation in ViewModel/saveable key. Use `NavigationSuiteScaffold` if a top-level rail/bar is needed. Current guidance: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/layouts/adaptive/get-started-with-adaptive-apps

TAILSCALE
- Phone: official Tailscale Android supports Android 8+, phones/tablets/Android TV; Android OS can keep a VpnService always-on from boot. Our app can run normally over it. Only one VPN service per user/profile can be active, so Tailscale conflicts with another VPN app, not with our ordinary app: https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/connectivity/vpn
- Watch blocker: as of July 2026, Tailscale’s official platform/download docs list Android but its Android install docs only name phones, tablets and Android TV; no Wear OS/watch support is documented: https://tailscale.com/download and https://tailscale.com/docs/install/android . Do not make direct watch→home Tailscale a requirement. Unsupported APK sideloading is not an architecture.

ONE ARCHITECTURE
Home desktop is source of truth. It sends encrypted, high-priority FCM data only for red events; exposes authenticated REST/WebSocket on Tailscale. Native phone app uses Room/outbox + Flow/Compose; it validates FCM payloads, stores the event, posts a scrollable NotificationCompat notification with one RemoteInput action (2–4 choices + free-form), and retries idempotent answers to desktop over Tailscale. Wear OS receives the bridged notification; no watch APK in v1 IF <=1,024-character watch text is an accepted validated contract. Foreground app streams all colors/state/transcripts via Tailscale WebSocket. Optional fallback is self-hosted ntfy as UnifiedPush distributor. If arbitrary full-length watch text is mandatory, add a minimal native Wear Compose companion screen over Data Layer; do not jump to Flutter/RN or direct watch Tailscale.

## Out-of-renderer resolution recipe

Cheapest, most reliable:

1. Open `~/.superset/host/&lt;orgId&gt;/host.db` read-only → `SELECT agent_session_id FROM terminal_agent_bindings WHERE terminal_id = ?` (join `terminal_sessions` on `status='active'` for liveness — that's what `persistence.ts:72-99` does), or call `terminalAgents.list` over tRPC with the PSK.
2. Glob `~/.claude/projects/*/&lt;agent_session_id&gt;.jsonl` (Codex: `~/.codex/sessions/**/rollout-*-&lt;id&gt;.jsonl`).

Fallbacks: `~/.superset/agent-subagent-running/&lt;terminalId&gt;.teamstate.json` → `.path` gives the transcript directly (when present); or scan `~/.superset/session-pane-map/*.json` for `terminalId` match. Pending-question state only from `~/.superset/agent-subagent-running/&lt;terminalId&gt;.askq/` file existence.

Nothing modified.</result>

Path minted at `apps\desktop\src\main\lib\host-service-coordinator.ts:674` (`HOST_DB_PATH: path.join(organizationDir, "host.db")`) and `packages\cli\src\lib\host\manifest.ts:75-77`; consumed via `packages\host-service\src\env.ts:12` → `packages\host-service\src\serve.ts:57` → `packages\host-service\src\db\db.ts:10-27`. Sibling `manifest.json` (`manifest.ts:29-31`) carries `{ pid, endpoint, authToken, startedAt, organizationId }` — that is how a headless bridge discovers the tRPC endpoint + token.

**Tables** (`packages\host-service\src\db\schema.ts`): `terminal_sessions` (L16), `terminal_agent_bindings` (L46), `projects` (L65), `host_settings` (L98), `pull_requests` (L105), `host_agent_configs` (L151), `workspaces` (L179), `acp_sessions` (L231), `workspace_cloud_deletes` (L253). Migrations `packages\host-service\drizzle\0000…0011`.

Nothing in this schema stores snooze, archive, pin, section, or tab order.

---

## 2. Sidebar state — where snooze/archive/completed/deleted/pin/order actually live

**Renderer localStorage. Not host sqlite. Not Electric.**

Storage declaration: `apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\providers\CollectionsProvider\collections.ts`
- L781-795: `v2WorkspaceLocalState = createIndexedCollection(localStorageCollectionOptions(withReadHeal({ id: \`v2_workspace_local_state-${organizationId}\`, storageKey: \`v2-workspace-local-state-${organizationId}\`, … })))`
- L768-775: `v2SidebarProjects`, storageKey `v2-sidebar-projects-${organizationId}` (holds `isPinned`, `tabOrder`, `isCollapsed`, `show*/`*Collapsed` flags).
- L809-816: `v2SidebarSections`, storageKey `v2-sidebar-sections-${organizationId}`.
- Contrast L102-130 + L284-751: only the **Electric/cloud** collections go through `createPersistedElectricCollection` → `@tanstack/electron-db-sqlite-persistence`. The sidebar collections do not.

Field schema: `…\CollectionsProvider\dashboardSidebarLocal\schema.ts:139-207` — `workspaceLocalStateSchema.sidebarState` = `{ projectId, tabOrder, sectionId, changesFilter, changesViewMode, activeTab, isHidden, archivedAt, snoozeUntil, snoozeLaunchId, completedAt, deletedAt }`; plus top-level `paneLayout`, `viewedFiles`, `recentlyViewedFiles`, `workspaceRunTerminals`, `pendingMigratedTerminals`. Pin lives at L18 of the project schema (`isPinned`).

**Writers** (single file, all `collections.v2WorkspaceLocalState.update/insert`) — `apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\hooks\useDashboardSidebarState\useDashboardSidebarState.ts`:
- `archiveWorkspace` L556-599 (`archivedAt = Date.now()`, clears snooze) — L577-588 inserts a pre-archived row for auto-included mains.
- `snoozeWorkspace` L601-650 — `snoozeUntil = until` or `snoozeLaunchId = APP_LAUNCH_ID` (SNOOZE-MAIN insert path L610-636).
- `unsnoozeWorkspace` L652-661, `unsnoozeAllInProject` L663-677, `unarchiveWorkspaces` L683-694.
- `deleteWorkspace` L709-779 (`deletedAt`), `restoreWorkspace` L785-798.
- `completeWorkspace` L806-836 / `uncompleteWorkspace` L838-848 (`completedAt`; set only by the kanban board).
- `togglePinProject` L279-287 (`v2SidebarProjects.isPinned`), `reorderProjects` L289-299, `reorderWorkspaces` L301-312, `reorderProjectChildren` L314-338, `moveWorkspaceToSection(AtIndex)` L340-372 / L434-473 — all manual drag order → `tabOrder`.
- `(REMOVE-STICKY)` tombstones: `…\useDashboardSidebarState\sidebarMutations.ts:34-63` (`tombstoneSidebarWorkspaceRecord`, sets `isHidden`) and L88-117 (`removeProjectFromSidebarState`), passive-mount guard at `useDashboardSidebarState.ts:241-264`; passive mounts at `…\v2-workspace\layout.tsx:88` and `V2WorkspaceMount.tsx:76`.

Classifier `getWorkspaceSidebarBucket` — `…\CollectionsProvider\dashboardSidebarLocal\sidebarVisibility.ts:146-175`: precedence `deleted → completed → archived → snoozed → hidden → active`. `isWorkspaceArchived` L114-125 needs the host row's `type` ("main" stays merely hidden). `APP_LAUNCH_ID` L72 is a per-process `crypto.randomUUID()` — **"snooze until next launch" is meaningless outside the renderer process that minted it**; a headless reader sees an opaque UUID and cannot tell whether it matches the live app launch.

`(SIDEBAR-STATE-PROJECTION)` — `…\DashboardSidebar\hooks\useDashboardSidebarData\useDashboardSidebarData.ts:288` (carries a fork's full sidebar state), with the projection query at L241-268 (selects `snoozeUntil/snoozeLaunchId/archivedAt/completedAt/deletedAt` out of the localStorage collection) and the join to host rows at L269-305 — sidebar rows only exist where a local-state row **and** a host row both exist.

**Answer to the crux**: a headless process cannot read snooze/archive/pin/order. There is no sqlite table, no tRPC procedure, and no exported file. The only persistence is Chromium `localStorage` inside the Electron renderer's profile (Local Storage leveldb under Electron `userData`), keyed `v2-workspace-local-state-&lt;orgId&gt;` / `v2-sidebar-projects-&lt;orgId&gt;` / `v2-sidebar-sections-&lt;orgId&gt;`. The only sidebar-adjacent state ever written to a real file is the kanban board's daily append-only JSON snapshot: `apps\desktop\src\lib\trpc\routers\window.ts:143-204` → `~/.superset/backups/kanban/&lt;org&gt;-&lt;YYYY-MM-DD&gt;.json` (kanban cards carry their own `snoozeUntil/archivedAt/completedAt/deletedAt`, `schema.ts:542-589` — a stale, once-daily, kanban-only mirror, not the sidebar tree). Electric collections persist to `~/.superset/tanstack-db.sqlite` (`apps\desktop\src\main\lib\persistence\persistence.ts:139-158`, dir from `apps\desktop\src\main\lib\app-environment.ts:8-9`) but no sidebar collection is in it.

Corroboration that this gap is real today: `apps\mobile` has **zero** matches for `snooze`, `archivedAt`, or `sidebarState` — the existing phone client reproduces workspaces from host data only.

---

## 3. Tiering / sort

`apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\_dashboard\components\DashboardSidebar\DashboardSidebar.tsx`
- L54-64 `(ACTIVE-FIRST)`:
  ```ts
  function getProjectTierRank(project: DashboardSidebarProject): number {
  	if (project.isPinned) return 0;
  	return getProjectChildrenWorkspaces(project.children).length &gt; 0 ? 1 : 2;
  }
  ```
- L185-205: stable 3-tier partition applied over the manual drag order (`projectOrder`, seeded from `groups` at L147-152).
- L157-183: the currently-open workspace explicitly does **not** affect sort.
- `(HOVER-FREEZE)` L216 + L230-253: pointer-inside detection via rAF-throttled bounding-rect test; `frozenOrderRef` (L233) holds the last order while the pointer is over the list, re-applied on leave; tier-change re-evaluation at L303.
- Tier field plumbed through `types.ts:93-95` and `buildDashboardSidebarProjects.ts:22`.

**"Active" is NOT dot-derived.** It is `children.length &gt; 0` where `children` are the workspaces bucketed `active` by `getWorkspaceSidebarBucket` (`useDashboardSidebarData.ts:361-380`) — i.e. host workspace rows minus renderer-local snooze/archive/complete/delete. So the tier is computable headlessly *only if* you also have the sidebar-state layer; the host half (which workspaces exist per project) is fully available, the subtractive half is not.

The dot/status layer is separately renderer-only: `apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts` — Zustand persisted to `window.sessionStorage` under name `v2-notification-dots` (L630-639), `agentTerminals` registry L168, L335-355, L389-410, `(AGENT-SHELL-BLUE)` gate L849-862. Fed by main-process hook events, not queryable.

---

## 4. Terminals per workspace, agent vs plain shell

Two independent sources; the sidebar joins them.

**Host-side (headless-readable):**
- Which terminals are alive: `terminal.listSessions` (above), merging in-memory attachment state with `terminal_sessions` rows the daemon still has alive (`terminal.ts:596-656`; dispose-stamped rows excluded L644).
- Which of those are agents: `terminalAgents.listByWorkspace` / `list`, backed by `terminal_agent_bindings` joined to `terminal_sessions` where `status != 'disposed'` (`packages\host-service\src\terminal-agents\persistence.ts:46-60`). A terminal with a binding is an agent terminal; without, a plain shell. `lastEventType` gives working/permission.
- `packages\host-service\src\db\schema.ts:46-63` — bindings carry `agentId`, `definitionId`, `agentSessionId`, `startedAt`, `lastEventAt`, `lastEventType`.

**Renderer-side:**
- The tab/terminal *tree the sidebar renders* comes from `paneLayout` inside `v2WorkspaceLocalState` — localStorage. `apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\v2-notifications\store.ts:897-920` (`useV2WorkspaceTabChips` live-queries `local.paneLayout`), consumed by `…\DashboardSidebarWorkspaceDetails\hooks\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceTabChips\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceTabChips.ts:21-53` `(TAB-CHIPS)` which joins pane `terminalId` → host binding for the agent icon, and takes `status` from the renderer dot store, explicitly *"never host session status or raw agent lifecycle events"* (L16-20).
- Writer: `apps\desktop\src\renderer\stores\workspace-creates\writeWorkspacePaneLayout.ts:23-74`.
- Host bindings reach the renderer via `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-service\useTerminalAgentBindings\useTerminalAgentBindings.ts:33-53` (query + `agent:lifecycle` / `terminal:lifecycle` invalidation).
- Running-agent chips: `…\DashboardSidebarWorkspaceChips\hooks\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceRunningAgents\useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceRunningAgents.ts:45-56` (host bindings ⨯ renderer dot statuses).
- `agentTerminals` (`store.ts:168`) is a renderer sessionStorage latch used only for the blue-shell gate — not a source of truth for agent-ness; the host bindings are.

**For a phone client**: the *open-tab* view is unreachable headlessly, but "which terminals exist and which are agents" is fully reachable — `terminal.listSessions` + `terminalAgents.list`. Precedent: `apps\mobile\screens\(authenticated)\(home)\home\hooks\useHostTerminalAgents\useHostTerminalAgents.ts:29-67` already derives per-workspace `working`/`permission` from `terminalAgents.list` `lastEventType` on a 5s poll (mirrors desktop's `deriveTerminalAgentStatus`).

---

## 5. `v2WorkspaceLocalState` backing store

- Backed by `localStorageCollectionOptions` from `@tanstack/react-db` (`collections.ts:45`, used at L782), **not** Electric, **not** the Electron sqlite persistence adapter (which is applied only at `collections.ts:118-130` to Electric collections).
- `storageKey`: `v2-workspace-local-state-${organizationId}`; key fn `item.workspaceId` (`collections.ts:786-790`). Read-time heal `healWorkspaceLocalState` (`dashboardSidebarLocal\schema.ts:416-445`) merges `SIDEBAR_STATE_DEFAULTS` (L212-224) so pre-existing rows read back normalized.
- Three in-memory indexes on `sidebarState.projectId` / `sectionId` / `tabOrder` (`collections.ts:796-807`).
- **Persisted file a headless reader could open: none that is usable.** The bytes sit in the Electron renderer's Chromium `Local Storage` leveldb (app `userData`), which is a private, lock-held, format-unstable store — no export, no IPC read path, no mirror in `host.db` or `~/.superset/tanstack-db.sqlite`.

---

## Bridge implication (one line each)

- Repo rows, branch workspaces, terminals, agent-vs-shell, per-agent working/permission: **available headlessly today** via `project.list`, `workspace.list`, `terminal.listSessions`, `terminalAgents.list`, plus `workspace:changed` / `agent:lifecycle` / `terminal:lifecycle` WS events; endpoint+token from `~/.superset/host/&lt;org&gt;/manifest.json`.
- Pinned/active/idle tiering, snooze, archive, completed, recycle bin, sections, manual drag order, pane/tab layout: **renderer-only**, so the bridge needs either (a) a new host-side table + procedures owning sidebar state, or (b) a renderer-resident exporter pushing `v2WorkspaceLocalState` / `v2SidebarProjects` / `v2SidebarSections` out (which makes the phone view dark whenever the desktop app is closed). Note `APP_LAUNCH_ID` snoozes cannot survive relocation as-is — they'd need to become absolute timestamps or a host-owned launch epoch.</result>

<result># Host-service tRPC from the Electron renderer

## 0. Three distinct tRPC clients — don't mix them

| Client | Import | Transport | Style |
|---|---|---|---|
| **host-service** | `renderer/lib/host-service-client` → `getHostServiceClientByUrl(hostUrl)` | HTTP to `${hostUrl}/trpc` | **vanilla** client → `.query()` / `.mutate()` |
| Electron main IPC | `renderer/lib/electron-trpc` → `electronTrpc` | IPC | `createTRPCReact` → `.useQuery()` / `.useMutation()` |
| Cloud/web API | `renderer/lib/api-trpc-client` → `apiTrpcClient` | HTTP `${NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL}/api/trpc` | vanilla proxy client |

Alias: `apps/desktop/tsconfig.json` line 5-8 → `"*": ["./src/*"]`, so `renderer/lib/...` = `apps/desktop/src/renderer/lib/...`.

**There is no `useHostServiceClient`, `hostTrpc`, `trpcHost`, or `createTRPCReact` for host-service anywhere in the repo** (grepped `apps` + `packages`, zero hits). The host-service client is not a React hook — you call it inside a react-query `queryFn`/`mutationFn`.

---

## 1. The client factory

`C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\apps\desktop\src\renderer\lib\host-service-client.ts` (whole file, 33 lines):

```ts
import type { AppRouter } from "@superset/host-service";
import { createTRPCClient, httpLink } from "@trpc/client";
import superjson from "superjson";
import { getHostServiceHeaders } from "./host-service-auth";

const clientCache = new Map&lt;
	string,
	ReturnType&lt;typeof createTRPCClient&lt;AppRouter&gt;&gt;
&gt;();

export type HostServiceClient = ReturnType&lt;typeof createTRPCClient&lt;AppRouter&gt;&gt;;

export function getHostServiceClient(port: number): HostServiceClient {
	return getHostServiceClientByUrl(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}`);
}

export function getHostServiceClientByUrl(hostUrl: string): HostServiceClient {
	const cached = clientCache.get(hostUrl);
	if (cached) return cached;

	const client = createTRPCClient&lt;AppRouter&gt;({
		links: [
			httpLink({
				url: `${hostUrl}/trpc`,
				transformer: superjson,
				headers: () =&gt; getHostServiceHeaders(hostUrl),
			}),
		],
	});

	clientCache.set(hostUrl, client);
	return client;
}
```

`AppRouter` comes from `packages/host-service/src/index.ts` line 28: `export type { AppRouter } from "./trpc/router";` — package name `@superset/host-service`. Router mount table: `packages/host-service/src/trpc/router/router.ts` lines 33-62 (`workspace`, `workspaces`, `workspaceCleanup`, `workspaceCreation`, `project`, `ports`, `settings`, `git`, `terminal`, `companion` (fork-only), …).

Auth headers (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/lib/host-service-auth.ts`): local host → `Authorization: Bearer &lt;PSK secret&gt;` + `x-superset-client-machine-id`; relay → user JWT. Secrets are registered by `LocalHostServiceProvider` via `setHostServiceSecret(hostUrl, secret)`.

### 2c. Query + mutation pair, both keyed on `hostUrl`
`...\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\settings\components\V2WorktreeLocationPicker\useV2WorktreeLocationSettings.ts` (whole file, 44 lines):
```ts
import { toast } from "@superset/ui/sonner";
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { getHostServiceClientByUrl } from "renderer/lib/host-service-client";

export function v2WorktreeLocationQueryKey(hostUrl: string | null) {
	return ["host-settings", "worktree-location", hostUrl] as const;
}

export function useV2WorktreeLocationSettings(
	hostUrl: string | null,
	opts?: { enabled?: boolean },
) {
	return useQuery({
		queryKey: v2WorktreeLocationQueryKey(hostUrl),
		enabled: Boolean(hostUrl) &amp;&amp; (opts?.enabled ?? true),
		queryFn: async () =&gt; {
			if (!hostUrl) throw new Error("Host unavailable");
			return getHostServiceClientByUrl(
				hostUrl,
			).settings.worktreeLocation.get.query();
		},
	});
}

export function useSetV2WorktreeBaseDir(hostUrl: string | null) {
	const queryClient = useQueryClient();
	return useMutation({
		mutationFn: async (path: string | null) =&gt; {
			if (!hostUrl) throw new Error("Host unavailable");
			return getHostServiceClientByUrl(
				hostUrl,
			).settings.worktreeLocation.set.mutate({ path });
		},
		onSuccess: (data, path) =&gt; {
			queryClient.setQueryData(v2WorktreeLocationQueryKey(hostUrl), data);
			toast.success(
				path ? "Worktree location updated" : "Worktree location reset",
			);
		},
		onError: (err) =&gt; {
			toast.error(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
		},
	});
}
```

## 3. Per-host, always. How a component gets `hostUrl`

Host-service calls are **per-host**. Every call needs a resolved `hostUrl` string. Four resolvers, in order of specificity:

**(a) Local host only** — `useLocalHostService()` from `apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\providers\LocalHostServiceProvider\LocalHostServiceProvider.tsx`:
```ts
import { useLocalHostService } from "renderer/routes/_authenticated/providers/LocalHostServiceProvider";
const { machineId, activeHostUrl, activeOrganizationId, hostServiceStatus, waitForHostReady } = useLocalHostService();
```
`activeHostUrl` = `http://127.0.0.1:${connection.port}` (line 164), `null` until the local host boots. Port + PSK come from Electron main over `electronTrpc.hostServiceCoordinator.getConnection.useQuery` (lines 76-80). `waitForHostReady(timeoutMs?)` (lines 102-141) resolves the URL on demand.

**(b) By workspace id** — `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-service\useWorkspaceHostUrl\useWorkspaceHostUrl.ts`:
```ts
export type WorkspaceHostTarget =
	| { status: "loading" }
	| { status: "not-found" }
	| { status: "local-starting"; hostId: string }
	| { status: "ready"; kind: "local" | "remote"; hostId: string; url: string };

export function useWorkspaceHostTarget(workspaceId: string | null): WorkspaceHostTarget
export function useWorkspaceHostUrl(workspaceId: string | null): string | null
```
Remote branch (lines 45-51):
```ts
		const routingKey = buildHostRoutingKey(match.organizationId, match.hostId);
		return {
			status: "ready",
			kind: "remote",
			hostId: match.hostId,
			url: `${relayUrl}/hosts/${routingKey}`,
		};
```
It resolves the workspace via `useHostWorkspaces()` (line 26).

**(c) By hostId** — `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-service\useHostTargetUrl\useHostTargetUrl.ts`:
```ts
export function useHostUrl(hostId: string | null | undefined): string | null
export function useHostUrls(hostIds: string[]): Array&lt;{ hostId: string; url: string | null; isLocal: boolean }&gt;
```
Core rule (lines 26-33):
```ts
function resolveUrl(
	hostId: string | null,
	{ machineId, activeHostUrl, activeOrganizationId, relayUrl }: HostUrlContext,
): string | null {
	if (hostId === null || hostId === machineId) return activeHostUrl;
	if (!activeOrganizationId) return null;
	return `${relayUrl}/hosts/${buildHostRoutingKey(activeOrganizationId, hostId)}`;
}
```

**(d) Fan-out over all hosts** — `deriveHostWorkspacesQueryTargets` / `deriveHostProjectsQueryTargets`, described below.

---

## 4. Loading/error handling

- **`@tanstack/react-query` `useQuery` / `useMutation` / `useQueries` with a hand-written `queryFn`/`mutationFn`** is the dominant pattern. tRPC's own `useMutation` is **only** available on `electronTrpc` (main-process IPC), never for host-service.
- Errors surfaced with `toast.error(...)` from `@superset/ui/sonner`; pending via `mutation.isPending` (2b above).
- Plain async + typed error unions where a dialog needs retry semantics — see `useDestroyWorkspace` `normalizeError` (lines 131-157) which reads `err.data.teardownFailure` / `deleteInProgress` / `code === "CONFLICT"` off `TRPCClientError`, and consumer `...\DashboardSidebarDeleteDialog\hooks\useDestroyDialogState\useDestroyDialogState.ts` (`useState` for `error`, retry with `force: true` at line 128).
- QueryClient defaults (`apps\desktop\src\renderer\providers\ElectronTRPCProvider\ElectronTRPCProvider.tsx:15-26`) — **relevant**, host-service queries inherit them:
```ts
const queryClient = new QueryClient({
	defaultOptions: {
		queries: { networkMode: "always", retry: false },
		mutations: { networkMode: "always", retry: false },
	},
});
```
- Server side adds a 5s default timeout to **queries only** (`packages/host-service/src/trpc/index.ts:80-116`, `queryProcedure` + `meta.timeoutMs`); mutations use bare `protectedProcedure`.

---

## 5. Fork-specific: `(KANBAN-HOST-SOURCE)` + `useHostWorkspaces`

Marker registered in `FEATURES.md:46` and scoped to `apps/desktop/src/renderer` at `FEATURES.md:124`. Rule: every fork surface needing the workspace universe reads `useHostWorkspaces`, **never** the Electric `v2Workspaces` mirror.

**The hook chain:**
1. `apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\providers\HostWorkspacesProvider\HostWorkspacesProvider.tsx` — runs the fan-out **once**, shares via context. Consumer import:
```ts
import { useHostWorkspaces } from "renderer/routes/_authenticated/providers/HostWorkspacesProvider";
```
2. Source hook `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-workspaces\useHostWorkspaces\useHostWorkspaces.ts` → `useHostWorkspacesSource(scopedHostId?)`.

**How it reaches host-service** (lines 158-193) — `useQueries`, one query per host target, each calling `workspace.list.query()` on that host's client:
```ts
	const queries = useQueries({
		queries: targets.map((target) =&gt; ({
			queryKey: getHostWorkspacesQueryKey(target),
			enabled: target.hostUrl !== null,
			refetchInterval: WORKSPACES_FALLBACK_REFETCH_INTERVAL_MS,
			networkMode: "always" as const,
			refetchIntervalInBackground: true,
			retry: 1,
			queryFn: async (): Promise&lt;HostWorkspaceRow[]&gt; =&gt; {
				if (!target.hostUrl) return [];
				const client = getHostServiceClientByUrl(target.hostUrl);
				const rows =
					(await client.workspace.list.query()) as HostWorkspaceRow[];
				setLiveAnsweredHostIds((prev) =&gt;
					prev.has(target.machineId) ? prev : new Set(prev).add(target.machineId),
				);
				saveHostWorkspacesSnapshot(
					target.organizationId,
					target.machineId,
					rows,
				);
				return rows;
			},
		})),
	});
```
Server: `packages/host-service/src/trpc/router/workspace/workspace.ts:40` → `list: protectedProcedure.query(({ ctx }) =&gt; { ... })`.

**Target derivation** — `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-workspaces\useHostWorkspaces\useHostWorkspaces.utils.ts:56-98`:
```ts
export function deriveHostWorkspacesQueryTargets({
	activeHostUrl, hosts, machineId, relayUrl,
}: {...}): HostWorkspacesQueryTarget[] {
	const targets: HostWorkspacesQueryTarget[] = hosts.map((host) =&gt; {
		const isLocal = host.machineId === machineId;
		const hostUrl = isLocal
			? activeHostUrl
			: host.isOnline
				? `${relayUrl}/hosts/${buildHostRoutingKey(host.organizationId, host.machineId)}`
				: null;
		return { machineId: host.machineId, organizationId: host.organizationId, hostUrl, isLocal };
	});
	...
}
```
Query key (`useHostWorkspaces.utils.ts:39-49`): `["host-service", "workspaces", "list", target.machineId, target.hostUrl] as const`.

**Live updates**: WS event bus per host, not polling — `getEventBus(hostUrl, () =&gt; getHostServiceWsToken(hostUrl))` from `@superset/workspace-client`, listening `"workspace:changed"` (`useHostWorkspaces.ts:197-240`). Generic per-workspace version: `apps\desktop\src\renderer\hooks\host-service\useWorkspaceEvent\useWorkspaceEvent.ts` (`git:changed`, `fs:events`, `agent:lifecycle`, `terminal:lifecycle`, `port:changed`).

**Return contract** (`UseHostWorkspacesResult`, lines 40-67) — the fork-only absence-authority API you must respect:
```ts
	workspaces: HostWorkspaceItem[];
	isReady: boolean;
	isAuthoritative: boolean;                                   // (KANBAN-HOST-SOURCE)
	isAbsenceAuthoritative: (hostId: string | null | undefined) =&gt; boolean; // (KANBAN-HOST-SOURCE)
	cache: HostWorkspacesCacheOps;
```
`cache` (lines 25-38) is the optimistic-write surface: `resolveHostUrl(hostId)`, `upsertWorkspace(row)`, `removeWorkspace(hostId, workspaceId)`, `invalidateHost(hostId)`.

## Minimal template to write against

```ts
import { useMutation, useQuery, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { toast } from "@superset/ui/sonner";
import { getHostServiceClientByUrl } from "renderer/lib/host-service-client";
import { useWorkspaceHostUrl } from "renderer/hooks/host-service/useWorkspaceHostUrl";
// or: import { useHostUrl } from "renderer/hooks/host-service/useHostTargetUrl";
// or: import { useLocalHostService } from "renderer/routes/_authenticated/providers/LocalHostServiceProvider";

export function useThing(workspaceId: string) {
	const hostUrl = useWorkspaceHostUrl(workspaceId); // string | null
	const queryClient = useQueryClient();

	const query = useQuery({
		queryKey: ["host-service", "thing", hostUrl, workspaceId] as const,
		enabled: Boolean(hostUrl),
		queryFn: () =&gt; {
			if (!hostUrl) throw new Error("Host unavailable");
			return getHostServiceClientByUrl(hostUrl).someRouter.get.query({ workspaceId });
		},
	});

	const mutation = useMutation({
		mutationFn: (input: { foo: string }) =&gt; {
			if (!hostUrl) throw new Error("Host unavailable");
			return getHostServiceClientByUrl(hostUrl).someRouter.set.mutate({ workspaceId, ...input });
		},
		onSuccess: () =&gt; queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["host-service", "thing", hostUrl, workspaceId] }),
		onError: (err) =&gt; toast.error(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)),
	});

	return { query, mutation };
}
```

Gotchas that will bite you:
- `getHostServiceClientByUrl` takes a **URL**, not a hostId or port. `getHostServiceClient(port)` is the loopback shortcut.
- `hostUrl` is `null` until the local host boots — always `enabled: Boolean(hostUrl)` and throw inside the fn; never call the factory with `null`.
- Always include `hostUrl` (or `machineId`) in the query key — otherwise two hosts share a cache entry.
- Set `networkMode: "always"` on anything that must work offline against `127.0.0.1` (the global default already does this; keep it if you override defaultOptions locally).
- New procedures: `.query` → `queryProcedure` (5s default timeout, raise with `.meta({ timeoutMs })`); `.mutation` → `protectedProcedure`.</result>

## Findings — status

**CRITICAL 1 — two state owners regress the nonce high-water mark → FIXED (anchor half) in `keys.ts`.**
New `StateAnchor` (`openStateAnchor`, `keys.ts:218`) is the sole writer of the send-nonce mark. `OPEN_ANCHORS` refuses a second open of the same path in-process. Every write is a read-modify-write against the FILE (`mutate`), not against memory, so the monotonic and ownership checks are statements about durable state. `claimSend` refuses a mark below the durable one; `raiseSend` refuses a stale `owner` token AND any lowering. `createSendNonceSource` POISONS itself on `StateRollbackError` and `next()` then throws — a stale owner cannot write and cannot keep emitting. `close()` passes `max(counter, reservedThrough)`, and the anchor refuses lower regardless of what a caller asks. **The lifecycle mutual-exclusion half is in `index.ts` — not mine; contract below.**

**CRITICAL 2 — state rollback repeats nonces → FIXED (bridge half) in `keys.ts` + `crypto.ts`.**
Anchor carries `generation` (minted once, stamped into every key file as `StoredKeyFile.anchorGeneration`, v2) and a monotonic `epoch` bumped once per mount and durable before anything derived from it is used. `KeyStore.load` throws `StateRollbackError` when a key file's generation ≠ the anchor's — key material and counter state from different installs can never be recombined. Every anchor/key write goes through `writeFileDurable` (`crypto.ts:820`): tmp → write → fsync → close → rename → `syncDirectory` parent fsync. Corrupt/unparsable anchor throws; there is no permissive branch. Legacy `send-nonce.json` is adopted into the anchor and only then retired, so migration cannot restart the counter under the same prefix.

**HIGH 3 — forward clock jump reopens the replay window → FIXED in `crypto.ts`.**
`monotonicNowMs()` (`performance.now`, `crypto.ts:782`). Retention is now `age AND insertion order`: `isExpired` returns false for anything inside the newest `REPLAY_MIN_RETAINED_ENTRIES = 8_192` (§12's 600 req/min × the 120 s window = 1 200 max admissible per window, so ~6.8× headroom), uses the monotonic age for records this process admitted, and treats a negative age as never-expired. `admit` now tests **presence only** — the old `nowMs - seen &lt;= retentionMs` re-admission was the specific hole. `rewrite` re-sorts by insertion order so `loadFromDisk` can treat the file tail as the age-exempt window across restarts.

**HIGH 4 — restored device index reverses revocation → key-material half FIXED in `keys.ts`; index half is `device-store.ts`.**
`KeyStore.markRevoked` / `revocationOf` write and read the tombstone **inside the key file**, a separate file from `devices.json`. §5.1 is preserved: the key survives so the device can still open its sealed 403. Anchor also carries `devices {seq, digest}` with `commitDevices` refusing a lower seq or a changed digest at an unchanged seq.

**MEDIUM 5 — corrupt nonce state failed open with a random prefix → FIXED in `keys.ts`.**
`readAnchorFile` throws `StateRollbackError` on unparsable/malformed/wrong-version/bad-generation/bad-epoch. A fresh random prefix is minted **only** when there is provably no prior send state. The error names the §3.4-rule-4 / §4.8 remedy.

**LOW — key buffers not zeroized → primitive landed, call sites NOT (see handover).** `zeroDirectionalKeys` added (`keys.ts:910`). Also added this pass: `assertDeviceKey` now rejects an **all-zero** K_dev. Without it, a wiped buffer is a valid HKDF PRK and derivation succeeds silently, presenting as an unexplained storm of `unknown_device` tag failures. Mirrors `x25519`'s existing all-zero rejection.

**NOT FIXED, not my files:** MEDIUM JWKS clock rollback (`access-jwt.ts`), MEDIUM ACL marker (`config.ts` — reassigned away from me mid-task), MEDIUM key-destruction orphan (`device-store.ts` purge), LOW JWKS size cap (`access-jwt.ts`), LOW unknown-device/bad-tag timing (`http.ts`).

## Handover — exact changes the other agent must make

**`index.ts`** (signatures I changed; the tree does not currently compile without these):
- `openStateAnchor(paths.root)` must be the FIRST resource in `start()`; add `anchor` to `BridgeState`.
- `createKeyStore(paths.devices)` → `createKeyStore(paths.devices, anchor)`.
- `createSendNonceSource(join(paths.root, "send-nonce.json"))` → `createSendNonceSource(anchor)`; drop that path (keys.ts migrates and deletes the file itself).
- Order: anchor → keyStore → sendNonces → deviceStore → nonceCache.
- `stop()`: `sendNonces.close()` before `anchor.close()`; anchor closes last.
- **A failed `start()` MUST unwind and call `anchor.close()`.** `OPEN_ANCHORS` keeps the path registered otherwise, and every retry throws "already open in this process".
- Lifecycle mutex: serialise start/stop from the first statement and gate on a phase flag. The anchor stops two owners corrupting *state*; it does not stop two half-built bridges.

**`device-store.ts`** — index format gains `{v, generation, epoch, seq, digest, records}`. At load, fail closed on: `generation !== anchor.generation`; `seq !== anchor.devices().seq` (either direction is a rollback); recomputed digest ≠ `anchor.devices().digest`; `file.epoch &gt; anchor.previousEpoch` (the anchor rolled back). `anchor.devices() === null` + legacy bare array → adopt via `commitDevices(1n, digest)`; `=== null` + a v2 file with records → fail closed; non-null + file missing → fail closed.
Digest over the **authority projection only**, sorted by deviceId: `{deviceId,label,surface,pairedAtMs,keyRef,fcmToken,writeEnabled,revokedAtMs,revokeReason}` — exclude `lastSeenMs`/`fcmTokenUpdatedMs` or per-request `touchLastSeen` forces an anchor round-trip on every request. Persist bumps `seq` + `commitDevices` only when that digest changed.
`revoke`/`revokeAll` must `await keyStore.markRevoked(keyRef, atMs, reason)` **before** persisting. At load, reconcile every record against `keyStore.revocationOf(keyRef)` and re-apply a tombstoned revocation loudly — that is the layer that survives an index-only restore.
Purge (fixes the orphan MEDIUM): `destroy(keyRef)` FIRST, then drop the record; on failure keep the record and log loud so the next purge retries. `KeyStore.destroy` is ENOENT-tolerant and idempotent, so retry is safe.

**`access-jwt.ts`** — import `monotonicNowMs` from `./crypto`; measure `snapshot.fetchedAtMs` and `lastFetchAttemptMs` with it, keep wall `now()` only for `exp`/`iat`/`nbf`, and treat a negative age as stale. For the size LOW: stop doing `await response.text()` then checking length — bound during read off `response.body`, and reject an over-cap `content-length` up front.

**Unassigned — flag to the coordinator:** `http.ts` / `ws.ts` own the two remaining LOWs. Zeroize: `zero(deviceKey)` after `deriveDirectionalKeys` (http.ts ~1203) and after `deriveEventKey` (ws.ts ~584) — both are the last use of that buffer, verified; `zeroDirectionalKeys(directional)` at request end; `zero(live.keyEvt)` on socket close. Timing: `openRequest` throws on `!device` before any key read, HKDF or GCM, so unknown-device and bad-tag do measurably different work despite identical wire output.

- `syncDirectory` is a **documented no-op on win32** — Node cannot open a directory handle there and NTFS exposes no user-space directory fsync. Content fsync and rename ordering still hold. This is the fork's own platform; recorded in the code, not papered over.

## What I changed (my four files)

**`index.ts`**
- `running` returned `state !== null` — true during teardown, false during a failed start. Now returns `phase === "running"`; the phase is the truth in both directions.
- Wired the required `onQuestionsSettled` dep that `read-api.ts` now demands (RECONCILE-RETRACT). Moved `push` construction **above** `readApi` so it is a real binding rather than a late `?.` that silently no-ops.
- Added `(ANCHOR-ORDER)` assert: the device store must have committed anti-rollback state before the send-nonce source exists. That ordering is what makes rollback detection work at all, so it is now checked instead of assumed.

**`device-store.ts`** — the coordinator's point, addressed properly
- The discarded `assertNoRollback` return is consumed. My earlier discard was exactly the "silence the type checker on a load-bearing check" shape they flagged.
- Adoption is now reported (`log.warn`) rather than absorbed — reaching it means the last run died between the index write and the anchor commit.
- Added `(ADOPT-COMMIT)` **post-condition**: after construction, the anchor must attest `(seq, digest)` equal to the set the store is about to serve — checked every start, not only adopting ones.

On your specific question: `persist()` **is** unconditional and **does** already commit the adoption (traced: index at N+1/D_new, anchor at N/D_old → `commitDevices(N+1, D_new)`), so step 3 of your scenario did not actually persist. But relying on that by inference is the fragility you named, so it is now a checked invariant that fires immediately if a future edit makes `persist()` conditional. And yes — the `keys.revocationOf` reconciliation is landed and unconditional, running over every record before the store is handed out, so it independently blunts step 4. Both are proven below; no belt-and-braces added beyond the post-condition.

**`access-jwt.ts`**
- JWKS cache freshness and the refetch interval were on `Date.now()`. Moving the wall clock **backwards** makes elapsed time negative, so the snapshot never expires and a retired Cloudflare signing key stays trusted indefinitely. Both are now monotonic. Init is `-Infinity`, not `0`, or the first genuine fetch would be rate-limited for the process's first 60 s.
- The size cap was `await response.text()` then check length — it buffered everything first. Now enforced during the read, with a `content-length` pre-check.

**`config.ts`** — and this one is live and severe
The shipped hardening call was `icacls &lt;root&gt; /inheritance:r /grant:r &lt;user&gt;:(OI)(CI)F /T`. `(OI)(CI)` are *inheritance* flags; on a leaf file they produce an **inherit-only** ACE granting nothing to the file itself, while `/inheritance:r` strips what it had. Measured on this machine: after that call, both secrets and every `*.key.json` return `EPERM to their own owner`. Since the operator must hand-place `cloudflare-access.json` before first start, the first real run bricked its own secrets and keys. The file now sets the template on the directory only, then `/reset /T` so existing children re-inherit (`(I)(F)`, this account only). I reproduced the failure and the fix side by side before accepting it. The `.acl-hardened` marker is gone — the DACL is re-read every start over root and `devices/`, drift re-applied and re-verified, unreadable ACL fails closed.

**1. Dead diagnostic surface: `SendNonceSource.state()` + `NonceState`**

**4. Dead type: `NonceCacheRecord`**
`types.ts:145`. Definition-only across whole repo — replay cache persists its own fixed-width binary records; this JSON-shaped record type is scaffolding from an earlier pass. Cost: describes a wire/disk format that no longer exists; actively misleading about how nonces persist. Simpler: delete.

**5. `NonceCache` adapter duplicates `ReplayCache` + stale comment**
`keys.ts:1439–1477` wraps `createReplayCache` solely to curry `Date.now()` into `admit`; two interfaces (`NonceCache`, `ReplayCache`) must stay in sync for one delegation. Cost: interface drift risk + a comment sending readers to fix a problem already fixed. Simpler: have `index.ts`/`http.ts` consume `ReplayCache` directly (callers pass `Date.now()`), delete the adapter, rewrite the crypto.ts comment to state the current relationship.

**6. `serialise` tail-promise helper copy-pasted 3×**
`keys.ts:284–291`, `crypto.ts:967–974`, `device-store.ts:433–440` — byte-identical. Cost: a fix to its error-chaining semantics (e.g. a rejected-tail leak) must be found and applied three times. Simpler: one exported `createSerialiser()` in crypto.ts (already the shared-primitives home), import twice.

**7. All-zero constant-time loop duplicated**
`crypto.ts:~331–341` (X25519 shared-secret check), `keys.ts:1218–1227` (`assertDeviceKey`) — same OR-accumulate loop, same explanatory comment. Cost: a subtlety fix (e.g. length handling) applied to one copy silently misses the other in security-critical code. Simpler: `export function isAllZero(bytes: Uint8Array): boolean` in crypto.ts; both call it.

**8. Byte-length/label constants re-declared per file**
`DEVICE_ID_BYTES` ×3 (pairing.ts:131, device-store.ts:82, +1), `X25519_KEY_BYTES` ×2, `KEY_BYTES` ×2, `MAX_LABEL_CHARS` ×2 (pairing.ts:137, device-store.ts:83). Cost: silent drift breaks the pairing→store handoff after the cryptographic acceptance point. Simpler: single source in config.ts (or types.ts), both files import; keep the dual *assertions* (boundary validation is deliberate) but share the *constants and predicates*.

**9. Low value: parallel anchor/witness file parse scaffolding**
`keys.ts` `readAnchorFile`/`readSendWitness` share the read → JSON.parse → field-validate → fail-loud shape with near-identical error plumbing. Cost: minor duplication; the two files genuinely differ in schema and failure policy. Only worth a shared `readJsonFileStrict` if a third durable file appears. No action needed now.

**Non-finding (verified, protocol-locked):** `pairing.ts:713–714` sets both `issuedAtMs` and `serverTimeMs` to `Date.now()`. PROTOCOL.md assigns them distinct roles — `serverTimeMs` is the client clock-sync sample (§3.6, lines 59–67, 462), `issuedAtMs` is what the desktop records per device (line 693). Same instant, different contracts. Keep both.

## Justified as-is

- The entire **StateAnchor + SEND-WITNESS** machinery in keys.ts (owner-token single-writer, generation/epoch, witness-before-anchor, mutate's re-read-from-disk) — deliberate anti-rollback design, per brief: untouchable.
- **device-store.ts** tombstone re-apply, ADOPT-COMMIT post-condition, pending-destroy journal, index-first-anchor-second persist ordering, and the authorityDigest projection (excluding only the two mutable-liveness fields is load-bearing, not accidental).
- **pairing.ts** PAIR-JUNK-SURVIVAL budget split (`confirmAttempts` vs `unattributedAttempts` vs windowed MAC failures) and every key-zeroing path.
- **access-jwt.ts** in full: the six fail-closed checks, monotonic JWKS cache, single-flight fetch, `readBoundedBody` streaming cap. It correctly imports `base64UrlDecode`/`monotonicNowMs` from crypto.ts rather than re-implementing — the copy-paste-with-variation failure mode the brief predicted did not materialize here.
- **Dual boundary validation** (pairing re-validating what device-store validates) stays — validate at API boundaries; only the shared constants behind it should be unified (finding 8).
- Explanatory WHY-comments throughout — deliberate; only the crypto.ts:885–889 comment is flagged (finding 5) because it is now factually superseded.

Net: the five files are structurally sound for their threat model; the yield is dead diagnostic surface from earlier passes (findings 1–4) and three mechanical dedups (5–8). Nothing found that would shrink the state machines themselves.</result>

# SIMPLIFICATION review — question-store.ts / index.ts / types.ts / config.ts

## Findings (ordered by value)

**1. `stop()` hand-mirrors the start-time unwind list — two teardown sequences to keep in sync**
`index.ts:281-595` (startInner builds `unwind: {what, close}[]`), `index.ts:602-654` (`stop()`).
`startInner` accumulates an unwind array per acquired resource, uses it only on failed start, then discards it. `stop()` re-writes the same close sequence by hand. Cost: adding a resource to startInner but forgetting stop() = silent leak on every clean shutdown; the failure is invisible (nothing asserts the lists match). Simpler: persist the unwind array into `state` on successful start; `stop()` replays it reversed. One teardown list, both paths.

**2. `captureFromHook` is dead and its comment is wrong**
`question-store.ts:634` (interface), `:1545` (impl), docstring `:642` "The real entry point".
Zero callers anywhere in host-service — the live entry is `asCaptureSink()` (wired in index.ts via `createNotifyingCaptureSink`). "The real entry point" describes superseded wiring; a reader tracing capture flow lands on the wrong door. Cost: dead public surface on the store's most security-sensitive path, plus an actively misleading comment. Simpler: delete method; move its validation note onto `asCaptureSink`.

**3. `toSummary` dead — forward-provisioning for an event stream deliberately not wired**
`question-store.ts:694` (interface), `:1710` (impl). Zero callers. `QuestionSummary` rides only `question.pending`/`question.stale` frames — never published (only `question.resolved` is, `index.ts:948`), and `events.ws` sits in `DELIBERATELY_UNGRANTED_CAPABILITIES`. Cost: must keep answerability/headline rules in sync in a method nothing exercises. Simpler: delete; reintroduce in the commit that grants `events.ws` — matching config.ts's own stated pattern ("add this token in the commit that supplies (1)").

**4. Write-only fields on `PendingQuestion` — diagnostic state dressed as custody state**
- `staleReason` — written `question-store.ts:592, :1526, :1537, :1640`; zero reads. Its only consumer would have been never-published `question.stale` frames.
- `hookTranscriptPath` — `:581, :1522`; zero reads. The hook-claim vs derived-path mismatch is already console.warn'd at capture (`:1491-1498`).
- `cwd` — `:583, :1523`; zero reads in either repo.

Cost: fields on the threat-model record look load-bearing; every change to eviction/reconcile/verify must reason about them for nothing. Simpler: drop all three; log `staleReason` at the `markStale` call sites instead of storing it.

**5. Five §10 error-detail interfaces enforce nothing**
`types.ts:226-247` — `AlreadyResolvedDetail`, `LeaseHeldDetail`, `GuardFailedDetail`, `CapabilityUnsupportedDetail`, `AccessDeniedDetail`. Never imported; answer.ts emits `detail` as untyped literals. Cost: the exact drift class the file header exists to prevent — documented shape vs emitted shape can diverge silently. Simpler (preferred over deletion, since they document protocol): apply at emit sites (`satisfies AlreadyResolvedDetail` etc.). One line per site.

**6. Declaration-only dead types/constants**
- `NonceCacheRecord` — `types.ts:145`; keys.ts uses its own shapes.
- `ConnectionState` — `types.ts:1169`; zero TS readers. Kotlin owns its own (`superset-companion/core/.../ConnectionState.kt` — verified, not deserialized from this type). TS copy is a doc mirror that can silently diverge from the real one.
- `PROTOCOL_0_OPERATIONS` — `config.ts:79`; zero readers — nothing actually enforces the protocol-0 op set from it.
- `HandleKind` member `"question"` — `question-store.ts:179`; `deriveHandle("question", ...)` never called (question IDs come from `mintQuestionId`).

Cost each: a reader must disprove relevance before every protocol change. Simpler: delete (or, for `PROTOCOL_0_OPERATIONS`, wire it into the op-dispatch assert if the enforcement was intended — currently it's neither).

**7. `TranscriptWindow.sawPartialTail` returned, never read**
`question-store.ts:787` (field), `:888` (set). Both consumers (`read-api.ts:848`, `question-store.ts:1682`) ignore it; the same fact surfaces per-entry via `newest.partial`. Simpler: drop from the return shape.

**8. Minor**
- Dead `??` fallbacks in `createNotifyingCaptureSink.resolve` — `index.ts:952-956`: after the `state === "resolved"` check, `resolvedAtMs`/`resolvedBy` are guaranteed set; fallbacks imply a reachable path that isn't.
- Dual start instants — `bridgeStartedMs = Date.now()` `index.ts:301` vs `startedAtMs = http.startedAtMs` `:596` (read by registry.ts:81). Two near-identical timestamps with different meanings a reader must distinguish; one captured instant serves both wire and registry.

## Justified as-is (verified, do not simplify)

- Double validation at the capture boundary and `QuestionCaptureInput`'s structural duplication of the sink shape — stated layering reason holds; the sink is the unauthenticated attack surface.
- Lifecycle phase machine + `exclusive()` serialization (BRIDGE-LIFECYCLE-OWNER).
- ACL-REVERIFY every start (no marker file) and the icacls drift description path.
- CAPTURE-BOUNDED/CAPTURE-EVICTION three-tier reclamation; live questions never evicted.
- Bounded transcript windowing (`readTranscriptWindow` limit checks, tri-state unreadable=refusal).
- PORT-BEFORE-STATE reservation.
- `DELIBERATELY_UNGRANTED_CAPABILITIES` + `assertCapabilityWiring` boot assert (`index.ts:1009`) — the withheld-capability machinery itself is load-bearing; only `toSummary` (finding 3) is the unwired remnant.
- `AnswerStatusResponse.recordsSinceMs`/`bridgeStartedMs` and `foreground: boolean|null` — documented, wire-consumed.
- Honest-failure states and explanatory WHY-comments throughout — only the `captureFromHook` docstring (finding 2) is wrong.

No finding removes a wire field any Kotlin client reads — all dead items are server-internal or ride frames never published.</result>

## SIMPLIFICATION review — ws.ts / push.ts / http.ts / read-api.ts / registry.ts / audit.ts / companion-question-sink.ts

### Context finding first (reframes everything in ws.ts)

**F0. Entire §9 event stream is dormant on BOTH sides — deliberately, and the code is honest about it.**
- Server: `events.ws` sits in `DELIBERATELY_UNGRANTED_CAPABILITIES` (`companion/index.ts:1004-1006`), `/v1/events/ticket` is gated on it (`http.ts:825`), and `createSnapshotSource()` throws by design (`index.ts:1339-1350`, `(EVENTS-WS-PRECONDITIONS)` comment `index.ts:1328`). No ticket can ever be issued → no socket ever authenticates → all 957 lines of `ws.ts` are unreachable in production.
- Kotlin: symmetric. `CompanionCore.eventStreamClient()` (CompanionCore.kt:98) — zero callers. `BridgeClient.eventTicket()` (BridgeClient.kt:256) — zero callers. Zero test coverage of `EventStreamClient`/`EventSequenceTracker`. `BridgeCompanionRepository.kt:~125-128` documents the decision: "The event WebSocket in `:core` is not wired up here… FCM wakes the app for the case that matters."
- Verdict: this is documented future scaffolding, not accidental dead code — the ungranted-capability registry pattern (born from the `push.fcm` sealed-501 incident, index.ts:995-1001) is exactly right. Not proposing deletion. But it means every ws.ts finding below is about *what ships when the capability is finally granted*, and the findings should be fixed BEFORE wiring, while nothing depends on them.

### Findings, ordered by value

**F1. http.ts — five parallel per-path tables, plus the unifier already exists but is dead.**
`SealedHandler` type (http.ts:181-184) and `RouteDefinition` interface (http.ts:186-192): grep-confirmed ZERO uses across both repos — leftovers of a superseded table-driven routing design. Meanwhile every route is described five times: `SCHEMAS` (:717-729), `ROUTE_CAPABILITY` (:814-826), `ROUTE_OPERATION` (:847-861), the `SealedHandlers` interface (:203-241), and a 60-line `dispatch` switch (:1331-1394) whose every case is the identical shape `{statusCode: 200, body: await h.X(ctx, body as XRequest)}`.
Cost: adding one endpoint touches five places; a missed entry in any one is a runtime hole (the `push.fcm` incident was exactly this class). Simpler form: one `ROUTES: Record&lt;path, {schema, capability, operation, handler}&gt;` table — i.e. resurrect `RouteDefinition` or delete it. Either way delete the two dead types now.

**F2. read-api.ts — `countStatuses` duplicates handleTree's status derivation verbatim.**
The `pending !== null ? "needs_input" : binding === undefined ? "idle" : statusFromEventType(...)` derivation appears at read-api.ts:535-540 (inside handleTree, :527-544) and again at :970-975 (inside `countStatuses`, :961-981).
Cost: a status-mapping change applied to one loop makes heartbeat counts disagree with the tree the phone renders — a silent divergence with no gate. Simpler form: extract one `deriveSessionStatus(session)` and have both call it.

**F3. read-api.ts — handler-level re-validation duplicating the http.ts zod pipeline.**
handleTree :501-508 (includeIdle/since), handleHello :364-375 (protocol range), handleHeartbeat :913-921, handleTranscript :822-837 (limit/before), and `requireCapability` :306-317 re-checking what `ROUTE_CAPABILITY` already gated. Now confirmed: NO bypass path exists — the ws snapshot source throws (F0), so every call arrives through the http.ts pipeline (zod schema at step 12, capability at step 13).
Cost: two validation surfaces that must be kept congruent by hand; a widened zod bound not mirrored here (or vice versa) produces confusing wrong-layer rejections. Simpler form: single source — either the zod schemas own bounds and handlers trust their input types, or (if defence-in-depth is wanted for the future snapshot wiring) say so in a comment; today's comments don't claim that.

**F4. ws.ts — ack machinery is dead even within the dormant design, on both sides.**
`lastAckedSeq`: field :283, init :627, sole update :722 — write-only, nothing reads it. Kotlin: `AckFrame` (Events.kt:282) and `WebSocketHandle.ack()` (EventStreamClient.kt:83) have zero callers; the repository comment explicitly disclaims an ack loop. PROTOCOL.md §9 requires the server to *accept* ack frames — it doesn't require remembering them.
Cost: bookkeeping that implies a redelivery contract nobody implements; a future maintainer will hunt for the reader. Simpler form: accept-and-discard the ack frame (keep the parse/refusal path — protocol-locked), delete `lastAckedSeq`. Protocol untouched.

**F5. ws.ts — per-socket `emittedEids`/`emittedOrder` dedup cache derivable from `highestGseq`.**
`EMITTED_EID_CACHE=2048` :84, structures :279-280, maintenance :333, :384-389. gseq is globally monotonic and gap-free, and every emit path (broadcast, ring replay :697-701, queued flush :704-709) ascends — one integer comparison replaces a 2048-entry Set+FIFO. CAVEAT that must survive the simplification: :683 sets `live.highestGseq = snapshot.tree.gseq` BEFORE the snapshot emit :684-694 — a naive gseq gate inside `emit` would drop the snapshot frame; set it after, or exempt the snapshot path.
Cost: cache-eviction logic, a tunable constant, and Set/array bookkeeping standing in for `frame.gseq &gt; live.highestGseq`.

**F6. Minor, each one-line.**
- ws.ts:113/:515 `EventTicket.issuedAtMs` — write-only (expiry uses `expiresAtMs`). Delete.
- push.ts:1277 `retract(questionId, _workspaceId)` — second param ignored (uses `record.workspaceId`); interface :914 forces callers to pass a dead arg. Drop from signature.
- ws.ts:869-872 inline header-lowercasing loop duplicates http.ts `headersOf` :1528-1534. Import it.
- ws.ts:126-132 `handleEventTicket` — one-line pass-through with a single caller (index.ts:484 lambda). Inline.

### Leads investigated and CLEARED

- **Sink `transcriptPath` is load-bearing** — not vestigial as the TRANSCRIPT-PATH-DERIVED comment first suggested: question-store.ts:1486-1522 compares the hook-supplied path against the derived one, rejects on mismatch, and retains it as `hookTranscriptPath` witness while storing the derived path. Correct as-is.
- **JSON `{"t":"pong"}` app-frames** — protocol-normative (PROTOCOL.md:1387-1388), client sends them. The dual liveness path (raw WS pong :651-653 + JSON pong :715-717/:789-793) is protocol-locked. Keep.
- **`maxConnections: 1` wire field** — normative (PROTOCOL.md:1322/1340). The hardcoded one-per-device replacement :571-577 is the real enforcement; constant only fills the wire field. Fine.

### Justified as-is — say so plainly

- **audit.ts** (186 lines): serialised fsync'd appends, rotation, retention — every piece earns its place; `hashJsonPayload` alive (answer.ts:85,3186; index.ts:58,724,1519,1620).
- **registry.ts** (83 lines): identity-checked process-wide handle. Clean.
- **push.ts** core design: dual-clock vouched sweep, sent-before-send commitment, RETRACT-WINDOW retention — all load-bearing against real failure modes (clock jumps, crash-between-send-and-record). Only F6's dead param.
- **companion-question-sink.ts**: CAPTURE-BOUNDED caps, GUARD5-ANCHOR derivation, module-level sink registration mirroring registry.ts — justified.
- **ws.ts core mechanisms** (single-use ticket, sealed per-socket frames, seq/gseq, snapshot-first, ring replay): protocol-locked and coherently built; the honest `DELIBERATELY_UNGRANTED` gate is the right way to hold unshipped surface. Findings F4/F5/F6 are the trims to make before granting `events.ws`.</result>

**1. lease.ts — four dead methods, verified unused (~45 lines)**
- `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\lease.ts:146` + 304–323 (`sweep`), `:122` + 262–264 (`get`), `:143` + 300–302 (`getMessage`), `:362` + 384–386 (`TerminalLockRegistry.isBusy`).

The six-guard transaction under one lock and guard re-evaluation before every keystroke; the two null-semantics readers (`readGuardSource` fail-closed vs `readVetoGuardSource`); guard-5's anchor floors, window squashing, and ascending-band check; `screenShowsPickerChrome`'s err-toward-matching bias; the durable attempt store's two-failure-mode open logic, tmp→fsync→rename writes, `recordsSinceMs` mount-time floor, and put/forget asymmetry; MESSAGE-ECHO's split submit with composer-echo differential and in-lock re-checks; `assertNoPickerOnScreen`'s three sources; lease refuse-not-queue, fallible `extend`, and the deliberate absence of `extendMessage` (the absence, not the duplication around it); the `runExclusive` queue-slot semantics; every keystrokes.ts byte-contract refusal, the `RawPtyWriter` brand + Promise probe, control-char policy sets; and `PROVEN_AGAINST` (no reader, but it is the declared anchor for the mandated canary test — not dead). Every act of curation the desktop performs — sidebar membership, project placement, soft-delete, archive, snooze, complete, hide, pin, manual order, and the open-pane set — lives in renderer `localStorage` collections (`v2WorkspaceLocalState`, `v2SidebarProjects`) that the bridge cannot read, and `host.db` has no column for any of it. The bridge's three read statements are therefore unqualified `SELECT ... FROM projects / workspaces / terminal_sessions` (`read-api.ts:158/161/167`), which on this machine ships **22/22 projects, 183/183 workspaces and 310 terminal rows** to the phone — not \"the sidebar plus some cruft\" but a structurally different, strictly larger set: 63 workspaces have no live terminal at all, 398 of the 405 `active` session rows are &gt;7 days old corpses whose panes were closed or whose host-service was killed (nothing reconciles `status='active'` against daemon liveness — the reaper only walks daemon→row, never row→daemon), 8 of them are frozen on `PermissionRequest` and render as permanent red \"waiting on you\", and soft-deleted / archived / snoozed / completed threads appear as ordinary live rows that can still fire a push. **UI level: the phone adds no compensating structure, and its one filter is broken three ways.** `toRows` (`AgentTree.kt:156`) flattens projects × workspaces into a single globally status-sorted list with no project grouping, no pinned tier, no sticky or collapsible headers, no age-out, a repo name reprinted on every workspace header, three redundant metadata labels per row, and the same filled-red container token on error chrome as on the one actionable question card. The single filter — the idle chip — defaults to showing everything (`SessionViewModel.kt:69`), filters *workspaces* while the list renders *terminals* (so idle siblings and plain shells survive \"Idle hidden\"), and on manual refresh is forwarded to the bridge, which prunes harder than the client does and overwrites the cache — so tapping Refresh with idle hidden permanently truncates the snapshot until the next `treeStale` heartbeat and zeroes the \"N hidden\" counter that was supposed to make the hiding safe. The probe proves all seven required properties plus two extras (resume settling; the registry seam) against the real `createPushSender` / `createPresenceStore` / `createPushFence` and a real migrated SQLite DB, with only the device store and `globalThis.fetch` faked (the JWT signing path runs for real against a generated throwaway key).

### Three places worth your attention

1. **`tmp/boot-bridge.ts` could not run** — blocked, not failing. Port 47610 is held by your live Superset (PID 19728), and the bridge correctly refuses to start a second instance rather than stealing the send-nonce counter. I did not touch your running app. It aborts at `reserveBridgePort`, which is *before* any of the new wiring, so it would not have exercised the fence or presence store anyway — I added probe block (8) to cover the registry seam it would otherwise have reached.

2. **Brief conflict I resolved rather than stopped on.** F2's formula is a pure `OR`, under which a `lock` beacon cannot fire a question held by a keystroke from &lt;60 s ago — but F6(b) requires firing "(including lock)" and F9 requires a "lock fires" test. Under strict `OR` those are unsatisfiable together. I made `locked` an **override** (a fresh locked beacon beats a fresh keystroke), documented in `presence.ts` under "THE ONE OVERRIDE", and proved it in probe case (5). Locking is a declared statement of absence; idleness is only inferred. Everything else follows F2 verbatim.

3. **One incidental line in `terminal.ts`.** Scoped `biome check --write` normalized a pre-existing formatting deviation (`isPowerShellReady`) two lines from nothing I wrote. I kept it so the file I touched passes lint, rather than committing a file that fails `biome check`.

Also worth noting: `retract()` had a fence leak I found in self-review — retracting a still-held (never-sent) question left its row behind, so a restart would reconstruct a hold the caller had explicitly given up. Fixed before commit. It currently has no product callers (`cancelPending` is the live path), so this was latent.

Environment note: the worktree had no `node_modules`, so I temporarily junctioned the main repo's trees in to run the gates, then removed all 31 junctions afterwards. Main repo verified intact.</result>

## Key decisions per item

- **1** — heartbeat is a `useState` beat + `setInterval(300_000)` in the effect deps; launch force compares `lastPushedLaunchIdRef` vs `APP_LAUNCH_ID`. **Deviation:** `APP_LAUNCH_ID` is *not* in the deps array — biome `useExhaustiveDependencies` rejects outer-scope module constants as extra deps and fails the lint gate. It's read inside the effect and the force is carried by the comparison, so behaviour matches the spec; the omission is documented inline and in the commit body.
- **1 (honesty note)** — the stated "after ANY reload the mirror holds the OLD launch id" mechanism doesn't hold on its own: a reload resets `lastSyncedSignatureRef` to null, so the first push of every launch already happens. The real load-bearing justification (which I wrote into the code) is that without a heartbeat `last_full_sync_at_ms` cannot mean liveness, so item 2's age-out is unimplementable. Item 1 is still required, for that reason.
- **2** — window 20 min = 4 heartbeats; boundary is `&gt;`; a *future* stamp is treated as fresh (a backward clock step is not a content problem). Both disable branches return one shared `passThroughCuration()`.
- **3** — `organizationId` is a **required** field on `CompanionMountInput` → `CompanionBridgeOptions` → `ReadDeps`; both mount sites pass `env.ORGANIZATION_ID` (validated non-empty in both env schemas).
- **7** — confirmed by reading the code that eviction *cannot* drop an armed buzz (`evictOldestSettled`/`prune` skip pending). Absent → `false` + `logger.error` naming the restart; settled → `false` silently. `PushSenderDeps.isStillUnanswered` docblock amended.
- **4** — gate runs **before** `schedule` so a suppressed question leaves no fence row for `reconstruct()`. Arms on: disabled curation, null workspace row, any throw (logged). Suppressions logged with verdict.
- **9** — cap = `min(10, ceil(0.25 × active rows))`. I used **`ceil`**, not `floor`: `floor` yields 0 for tables under 4 rows and would switch the walk off entirely there (it also breaks the existing 1-row two-pass test). Candidates sorted oldest-first; over-cap rows re-armed as absent-this-pass. Both-passes-listed threaded explicitly via a new `StaleRowPassState`; first pass after start begins `listed: false`.
- **10** — second map keyed on `row.projectId`; requirement stated on `deriveProjectKind`'s docblock.
- **11/8** — all new wire fields optional; `pinned` emitted ungated (display metadata), `questionCount`/`multiSelect` inherit the `tree.read` gate for free.
- **12** — two seams exported from `companion/index.ts` to make behaviour testable without booting a bridge (house style, matching `buildMirrorSnapshot`/`planStaleRowCorrection`): `createIsStillUnansweredProbe` (was an inline closure in `startInner`) and `armPush`/`isCuratedOffSidebar`/`NotifyingSinkDeps`. Behaviour unchanged. The kind test is discriminating — it fails with the old placement map.

<agent-message from="refusal-forensics">
FALSE REFUSAL — reproduced with the production matcher. Guard 5's row-band width check rejects any real AskUserQuestion picker because option descriptions push the numbered rows further apart than the guard allows. Question is still open on screen right now.

## 1. Message → code → guard

- Client copy: `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\superset-companion\app-phone\src\main\java\family\khaira\supersetcompanion\phone\model\AnswerOutcome.kt:96` — `GUARD_FAILED -> "The desktop refused: the question wasn't safely on screen"`
- Wire code: `guard_failed` / HTTP 412 (`core/.../protocol/Errors.kt:37`)
- Thrown at `packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:1803` (`sealed(412, "guard_failed", ...)`)
- Failing guard = **`screen`**, #2 of 6 in `GUARD_EVALUATION_ORDER` (answer.ts:248), class `load_bearing`
- Predicate: `matchPickerScreen` (answer.ts:748) → **`rowsFormAscendingBand`** (answer.ts:824) → `reason: "rows_out_of_order"`, `missing: ["row_order"]` (answer.ts:803-810)

What it checks: every option's `<digit><≤6 decoration chars><16-char label anchor>` must be found, AND the matched rows must sit within `rowWindows.length + SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK` overlapping 2-line windows (`SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK = 3`, answer.ts:550).

## 2. Evidence (all in `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\tmp\refusal-2026-08-08\`)

`host-service.log`, `host.db` + `-wal` + `-shm`, `dot-decisions.log`, `manifest.json`, `refusal-excerpt.txt`, `probe_transcript.py`, `probe_matcher.ts`, `probe_spread.ts`. All reads were read-only (`file:host.db?mode=ro`); nothing touched the live app.

  event: 'companion.answer.guard_failed',
  questionId: 'H5zpgMcynEX90kDgjO-UTw',

  keystrokeIndex: 0, written: 0,
