```
`written: 0` — nothing was typed into the terminal, so the refusal was clean.

`answer_attempts` newest row: request `1be1c219-…`, question `H5zpgMcynEX90kDgjO-UTw`, device `OVzqUZe2pNEB5QeKO_mZBw`, surface `phone`, created `2026-08-08 11:11:40` local, status `failed`, failure_code `guard_failed`, **`guards_passed_json = ["transcript"]`**.

`companion_push_fence`: armed+sent `2026-08-08 09:51:02` local, state `sent`, host_terminal `7c169fa8-f1c5-4cc2-b52f-1a9795cdeb57`, tool_use `REDACTED`, expires 15:51:02 — not expired.

`dot-decisions.log:3626`: `2026-08-08T08:51:02.96Z … eventType=PermissionRequest RED: permission (PreToolUse:AskUserQuestion)`, never cleared; last event on that terminal is 09:04:55Z.

No Android device attached (`adb devices` empty) — phone-side logs skipped.

## 3. Was the question genuinely on screen? Yes, three independent ways

1. **Transcript guard passed.** `guards_passed = ["transcript"]` means `toolResultExists` returned false — no `tool_result` for `REDACTED`. I confirmed directly: the `tool_use` is at transcript line 19227, ts `2026-08-08T08:51:02.665Z`, it is the **last assistant entry in the file**, and there is **no matching tool_result**. The agent is still blocked on it.
2. **`missing: ["row_order"]` and nothing else.** The `row_absent` branch (answer.ts:799) did not fire, so **all four option rows were found in the viewport snapshot**. The picker was rendered.
3. **Dot still red** on that terminal since the AskUserQuestion fired, never released.

The refusal was therefore caused solely by the *geometry* check, not by absence.

## 4. Root cause

The question is a 4-option picker whose options each carry a long description:
`Batch + judged remainder (Recommended)` / `Judged review of all 587` / `Leave them` / `Show me a sample first`.

Claude Code renders each option as a numbered row followed by its wrapped description, so consecutive numbered rows are 3-4 lines apart. Measured spread of the four rows vs the guard's budget (`probe_spread.ts`):

| terminal cols | row line indices | spread | allowed span |
|---|---|---|---|
| 80 | 0, 4, 8, 11 | 11 | 7 |
| 100 | 0, 4, 7, 10 | 10 | 7 |
| 120 | 0, 3, 6, 9 | 9 | 7 |

I ran the real exported `matchPickerScreen` against this exact item with a description-bearing render (`probe_matcher.ts`) and got the logged failure byte-for-byte at every width:
```
cols=80  WITH descriptions -> {"ok":false,"reason":"rows_out_of_order","missing":["row_order"],"digitMapped":true}
cols=100 WITH descriptions -> {"ok":false,"reason":"rows_out_of_order","missing":["row_order"],"digitMapped":true}
cols=120 WITH descriptions -> {"ok":false,"reason":"rows_out_of_order","missing":["row_order"],"digitMapped":true}
BARE consecutive rows      -> {"ok":true,"reason":"match","missing":[],"digitMapped":true}
```
`SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK = 3` assumes options render as consecutive lines. They never do when descriptions are present — which is essentially every AskUserQuestion.

**Not a 1.19.0 regression.** `git log -S` shows both `SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK` and `rowsFormAscendingBand` were introduced in the original companion commit `c43e25351` and never touched since. Nothing in the upstream merge (pane-map, renderer POST eventing, `.askq` markers, gseq) is involved — those guards were never reached. Not a staleness/restart issue either: the fence is live and the transcript guard passed.

The code says so itself, answer.ts:743-746: *"NOT YET CANARIED against real Claude Code picker output — the row-decoration allowance, the anchor lengths and the band slack above are the knobs a canary test would tune."* There are no tests over `matchPickerScreen` anywhere in `packages/host-service/src/companion/*.test.ts`.

Corroborating scale: `answer_attempts` holds 4 rows lifetime (the table is never pruned) — **0 confirmed, 4 failed**. The phone answer path has never once succeeded. The three 2026-08-05 failures have `guards_passed = []`, i.e. they died at the *transcript* guard — a separate cause, not investigated here.

## 5. Fix candidate

Primary: `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:824` (`rowsFormAscendingBand`), budget from `answer.ts:550` (`SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK`). The span budget must account for description and wrap lines per row rather than assuming one line each — e.g. a per-row allowance (`rows * MAX_LINES_PER_ROW + slack`, needs ≥ 4 at 80 cols, and descriptions run far longer than this sample's, so measure before picking).

Worth noting the same class of bug was already found and fixed in the sibling function `screenShowsPickerChrome` (answer.ts:889-898), whose comment records the measurements: *"a cap of two lines was measured to miss a picker whose first option label WRAPPED across three lines, and a picker with two blank lines between rows."* That fix was never carried across to guard 5. But do not simply copy it — `screenShowsPickerChrome` deliberately errs toward matching because a false positive there costs a refusal, whereas here a false positive types a digit into the wrong screen. The band exists as the (GUARD5-ANCHOR) anti-forgery property.

Better shape, worth considering over widening: match the option **descriptions** too. They are on screen, they are long, and they are bridge-visible from the capture — that verifies the whole picker block structurally and removes the need for rows to be near-adjacent, without weakening the forgery bar.

Either way this needs the canary test the comment asks for, built from a real captured picker snapshot.

</agent-message>

<agent-message from="guard5-fixer">
GUARD5-PICKER-GEOMETRY fixed and committed locally on main as **10be67f65** (not pushed). Files: `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts` and new `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\picker-screen.test.ts`.

**Shape chosen: the picker-block invariant (forensics' recommendation), not a per-row budget alone.** The rows must appear in ascending digit order, and *every gap between consecutive option rows must be explained by the render of the option above it* — either the gap is small enough to be wrap slack (`SCREEN_ROW_ADJACENT_SLACK = 3`), or that option's own description anchor (24 squashed chars, floored at `SCREEN_MIN_ANCHOR_CHARS`) appears on screen inside the gap AND the gap fits `descriptionLineBudget` = `ceil(squashedDescChars / 24) + 3`. So containment and a length-derived budget are both in, layered: containment proves the gap holds the right text, the budget stops a caller claiming more room than the text it must also put on screen can fill. The last row's trailing region is deliberately not examined — no following row bounds it, and requiring the last description to be fully visible would refuse a picker whose footer has scrolled while proving nothing extra.

Why this is a stronger anti-forgery bar than the band, not a weaker one: the band only asked "are these rows close together"; this asks "is the space between row N and row N+1 filled by the text option N is supposed to be showing". Placement is searched exhaustively (memoised DFS over each row's candidate windows) rather than greedily, because an upper bound on a gap means an early hit for one row can strand the next — an N-question prompt restarts its numbering at 1 for every question, so digits genuinely repeat on screen. Gap failures report a new `row_gap_unexplained` reason, kept distinct from `rows_out_of_order`, so the guard_failed log line stays diagnostic. `SCREEN_ROW_BAND_SLACK` and `rowsFormAscendingBand` are gone; the two doc blocks that named them are updated.

**Fixture provenance: LIVE CAPTURE, not a render.** The terminal was still open, so I read its viewport through the same path the bridge uses — `terminal.snapshot` on the tRPC router (`snapshotSession` → the per-session headless emulator), authenticated with the manifest Bearer token, probing `maxLines: 1` first to learn the row count exactly as `companion/index.ts` `snapshotScreen` does. Read-only; no write to the app or its DB. The result is 120 cols x 42 rows, unedited, embedded verbatim in the test. The item is built from the real `AskUserQuestion` `tool_use` payload pulled out of that session's transcript JSONL, so the needles are exactly the ones the bridge holds. The 80/100-column cases are renders of the same prompt in the layout the live capture proved.

The refusal is reproduced and closed on that exact fixture: running old and new matchers side by side over the captured viewport gave `OLD (band) → {"ok":false,"reason":"rows_out_of_order","missing":["row_order"]}` and `NEW (block) → {"ok":true,"reason":"match"}`. Root cause confirmed as reported — the four option rows sit on screen lines 16, 20, 23, 26 (a ten-line spread against an allowed seven) because each option's wrapped description is rendered under its row.

**Tests: 18 in the new file, 140 across the whole `src/companion` suite, 0 failures.** Positive (8): the live viewport for `requireOptionIndex` 0/1/2/3/null; the same prompt re-rendered at 80, 100, 120 cols; description-less options on consecutive lines; blank separator lines between rows; a label wrapping three lines; multi-select checkbox chrome; an N-question prompt whose two blocks share their first option label so digit 1 matches in both. Negative (9, all still refused): empty screen (`empty_screen`), idle composer (`anchor_absent`), prompt visible but rows gone (`row_absent`), descending rows (`rows_out_of_order`), rows scattered through unrelated output (`row_gap_unexplained`), a gap filled by the *wrong* option's description, a short description trying to buy a 20-line gap, a forged one-character-label item, and a real screen answered with an item carrying one-character labels (`anchor_too_weak`). Non-vacuity is proven two ways rather than asserted: against a mutant whose gap check always returns true, exactly the three gap negatives fail and nothing else; against a mutant that commits to the first placement, exactly the N-question case fails. A further assertion pins the fixture's own row line numbers so it cannot be silently swapped for an adjacent-row picker that would make every case pass for free.

Gate outputs, all green on the committed tree: `check-feature-markers.mjs` → "All 144 feature markers present" (144 is the real baseline, not 139; I added no marker to `FEATURES.md` — `(GUARD5-PICKER-GEOMETRY)` follows the existing `(GUARD5-ANCHOR)` precedent of an in-file token that the manifest does not list, so the count is unchanged). `check-dangerous-diagnostics.mjs` → "(REFERR-GATE) clean". `bunx tsc --noEmit` in `packages/host-service` → 2 errors total, both in `src/terminal/terminal.ts` (`hasRunningForegroundProcess` export, and an arity error at 2241), **0 under `src/companion/`**. `bunx biome check` scoped to the two touched paths → clean, no fixes applied, no warnings (I applied the optional-chain suggestion by hand rather than leave a warning). Staged blobs verified at 0 CR each. I touched none of the other five guards, the transaction, the lease, `keystrokes.ts`, or the attempt store.

**Secondary diagnosis — the three 2026-08-05 rows are not a second live guard bug.** They carry `guards_passed_json=[]`, `failure_code=guard_failed` and, crucially, `lease_id=NULL`, while the 2026-08-08 row that this task is about carries `guards_passed_json=["transcript"]` and a real lease id. On the current code every in-lock guard failure records `leaseId: lease.leaseId` (`answer.ts:1840`), so a NULL lease with a `guard_failed` code can only come from the outer catch at `answer.ts:2057-2079`, which records `status: failed`, `failureCode: error.body.code`, `guardsPassed: []`, `leaseId: null` for a `SealedError` thrown before the lease is taken. Two preflight throws use the `guard_failed` code: `assertWritableBinding` ("terminal has no agent binding", `answer.ts:1573`) and `requireHostTerminal` ("terminal has no workspace", `answer.ts:1592`). So those three are a binding/workspace preflight refusal — the phone re-tapping the same question 24s and 38s apart against a terminal the bridge could not resolve — and never reached the transcript guard, which had not run at all. Nothing to fix in the guard stack; if it recurs the honest improvement would be a distinct sealed code for preflight so `guard_failed` stops covering two very different situations.

**One genuine finding I did not fix, because it is outside this file and needs its own pty proof.** The live picker renders its free-text slot as **`5. Type something.`**, but `question-store.ts:399` derives `FREE_TEXT_OPTION_LABEL = "Other"` and guard 5 requires that label to be on screen at its own digit before pressing it. Every free-text answer from the phone is therefore refused with `row_absent` / `missing: ["freetext:4"]`. I pinned that behaviour in a test rather than changing it — the label is bridge-owned precisely so a caller cannot supply it, and `validateCapture` cross-checks any supplied slot against it, so retargeting it should be done deliberately and re-proven against a real pty. Regular option answers are unaffected: the free-text row is only required when its own digit is the one being pressed. The live capture also shows a `6. Chat about this` row that the capture contract knows nothing about, which is more evidence the picker's shape has moved since that contract was proven.
</agent-message>

[BLOCKER] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\question-store.ts:411

[MAJOR] ...\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:1277
Defect: `descriptionLineBudget` derives the permitted gap from the CLAIMED description length while only the first 24 squashed chars must be on screen. The documented invariant ("a caller cannot claim more room than the text it must also put on screen can fill") is false.
Failure: description is attacker-chosen (unauthenticated localhost hook). With `<24 chars copied off the victim's screen> + "x".repeat(4000)`, gaps of 8/20/41/120 windows all return `{ok:true,reason:"match"}` — wider than any viewport — because the budget is ceil(4059/24)+3=172 while the needle stays 24 chars. Same screen with the unpadded description is correctly refused at gap 8. The picker-block invariant collapses back to "each row's digit+label appears somewhere ascending", the property the band rule existed to prevent. The existing negative test pins only the unpadded direction.
Fix: cap the gap at a viewport-sized constant independent of claimed length, or budget from description text actually found on screen (one anchor per N lines of gap).

[MAJOR] ...\companion\answer.ts:972
Defect: for the LAST option row the pressed-row verification region is `[at, windows.length)` — the whole screen below it — so a short-labelled last option is verified by its description appearing anywhere beneath the picker.
Failure: two options, pressing option 1 ("Skip", description NOT under its row but 15 lines below in unrelated scan output) returns `{ok:true,reason:"match"}`; the mirror case with the description ABOVE the rows is correctly refused (`anchor_too_weak / option:1`). This is the highest-traffic instance, not a corner: the live failure that motivated the evidence tier was a fourth-and-last option labelled "Skip", so the weakest binding lands on exactly the row the tier was built for. Falsifies be95ab69c's claim that the pressed row's verification "cannot be satisfied by a coincidence outside the picker".
Fix: `to: Math.min(windows.length, at + descriptionLineBudget(option.description))`.

[MAJOR] ...\companion\answer.ts:1048
Defect: when the pressed digit is the FREE-TEXT row, `requireOptionIndex` (= options.length) matches no `option.index`, so rowIsVerifiedEnough returns true for every row; the "at least one strong row" fallback at answer.ts:844 is gated on `requireOptionIndex === null` so it does not run either. Zero strongly-verified rows.
Failure: an item of one-character labels — the exact forgery the anchor floor was built for — passes on the free-text digit while every other press on the identical item/screen is refused. On `1. A / 2. B / 3. Type something.`: option 0 -> anchor_too_weak/option:0, option 1 -> anchor_too_weak/option:1, null -> anchor_too_weak/row_evidence, free-text digit 3 -> `{ok:true,reason:"match"}`. Currently unreachable (encodeAnswer throws shape_unproven, FREE_TEXT_CONTRACT_PROVEN=false) so this is armed, not live — it fires when that flag flips, which is a planned change with no reason to revisit this file.
Fix: require a strongly-verified option row inside the accepted block whenever requireOptionIndex names no option, instead of keying the fallback on null.

[MAJOR] ...\companion\answer.ts:1120
Defect: SCREEN_FREE_TEXT_LABELS holds both "Type something." and "Type something"; the second is a PREFIX of the first and matching is substring-based, so the pinned set is a prefix match and "unknown copy fails closed" is lost for any append-only drift.
Failure: `3. Type something else entirely` is accepted as the editor slot (measured ok:true); `3. So the one thing the pinned set exists to distinguish is what it fails to distinguish, and typing free text into a real option is the irreversible wrong answer the guard cites as its reason to exist. Same latency as above.

[MINOR] ...\companion\picker-screen.test.ts:527 — "a forged item whose labels are too short to be evidence" refuses on the PROMPT anchor (`header:"c", question:"c"` -> promptAnchor "" -> anchor_too_weak/missing:["prompt"]), not on row evidence, and asserts only `.ok===false`; it would keep passing with the row-evidence rule deleted. [MINOR] ...\companion\answer.ts:844 — the `requireOptionIndex === null` fallback searches `from:0, to:windows.length`, so the one strong row it demands need not be inside the accepted block. Defect: the `requireOptionIndex === null` pre-check calls `rowIsStronglyVerified({ option, windows, from: 0, to: windows.length })`. Since 513303ac6 that function clamps its search to `Math.min(to, from + SCREEN_EVIDENCE_REGION_LINES)` — with `from: 0` that is windows **0..3**, the top four lines of the viewport. Description evidence is never there, so the check degenerates to "at least one option label is >= 8 squashed characters".

Failure: a multi-select AskUserQuestion whose options all carry short labels — `Yes` / `No` / `Later`, the exact shape (GUARD5-EVIDENCE-TIERS) exists to support — with real descriptions. `encodeAnswer` emits `[toggle "1", toggle "3", submit_tab "\x1b[C", submit_return "\r"]`. Executed against a rendered picker of that shape:

    ["Yes","No","Later"]                   toggle0: true  submit_tab: {"ok":false,"reason":"anchor_too_weak","missing":["row_evidence"]}
    ["Retire the duplicates","No","Later"] toggle0: true  submit_tab: {"ok":true,"reason":"match"}

The toggle digits pass guard 5 (each names its own row, strong via its own description) and are written to the pty. `submit_tab` carries `optionIndex: null`, hits this pre-check, and is refused. `injectSequence` aborts with `written === 2`, so `abort()` returns `unconfirmed`: the question is marked stale, the ledger records `unconfirmed`, and the live picker is left with rows 1 and 3 toggled and never submitted. No compensating keystroke by design, and the question cannot be re-answered. Before this batch the same prompt was refused at keystroke 0 with `written === 0` — a clean `guard_failed` — so the batch converts a harmless refusal into a partial write on a live picker.

Fix: delete the pre-check. `rowsFormPickerBlock`'s `sawStrongRow` already enforces "at least one option row strongly verified at the position the accepted placement gave it" for every call including the null case, with the correct per-row region (`rowIsOptionAndStrong(options, row, windows, at)`). If a pre-filter is kept it must iterate each option's candidate window indices from `rowWindows` instead of passing `from: 0`. Add a test for a multi-select picker with all-short labels PLUS descriptions asserting `requireOptionIndex: null` matches — the existing "an all-weak item with no digit pressed anchors on nothing" case uses empty descriptions, so it passes either way and is what let this through.

3. BridgeCompanionRepository.kt `handle` — the `is BridgeErrorException ->` arm became a block; it discards on `invalidatesCachedSession(e.error.code)`. Discard only, no auto-retry. Not added to the CleartextErrorException arm: neither code is in CLEARTEXT_PERMITTED, so a cleartext body carrying one throws ProtocolException at decode.

4. Errors.kt (core) — `SESSION_EXPIRED("session_expired", 409)`, sealed-only, deliberately absent from CLEARTEXT_PERMITTED. Tolerant if the bridge never sends it; without the enum entry it would decode to UNKNOWN and be shown as an internal error.

5. Mapping/copy for the new code: BridgeMapping.errorCode, phone `BridgeErrorCode.SESSION_EXPIRED` with userText "The desktop restarted — nothing was sent, try again" (deliberately NOT the "answer at the desk" copy), PushRegistration `registrationIsRetryable = true`, WearProtocol `WatchFailureCode.SESSION_EXPIRED`, WatchBridge mapping, and an AnswerStateScreen arm on the watch ("Desktop restarted"). An old watch APK hits explainFailure's unknown arm, which shows the detail verbatim and claims nothing about what was typed — so no watch reinstall is required.

ONE ADDITION BEYOND THE BRIEF (flagging for your veto): in `write()`, when the mandatory pre-write heartbeat is itself the thing that reveals the restart, the write is now refused BEFORE anything is composed — `AnswerOutcome.Failed(SESSION_EXPIRED, "the desktop restarted, so this phone reconnected — nothing was sent")` — instead of being sent against a session we have just proven dead. This is not a retry and adds no round trip: nothing leaves the phone, and the user's next tap re-hellos. Without it, that race (session cached, bridge restarts, the write's own beat sees it first) still costs one "answer at the desk" refusal. I did NOT re-hello inline there; that would put a second round trip on a path documented as one heartbeat, one attempt.

TESTS — SessionRehelloTest.kt (9 tests, all passing): first beat is not a restart; equal lifetime is not; changed lifetime is; a lifetime that moved BACKWARDS still is; capability_unsupported invalidates; session_expired invalidates; all 20 other wire codes do not; session_expired is 409 + sealed-only; and the new code does not reach the user as "unsupported desktop".
Honest limitation: these prove the decisions, not the class wiring. BridgeCompanionRepository takes a concrete CompanionCore, which needs the Android Keystore, so there is no JVM seam to drive recordHeartbeat/handle end to end — which is exactly why both call sites are a single `if` over these predicates. "A subsequent call re-hellos" is therefore covered by `session()`'s existing invariant (it hellos iff wireSession is null), verified by reading, not by a test.

<agent-message from="phone-session-fixer">
R1 + R2 fixed. New commit a6dcd98 on main (unpushed), on top of 9b9aae2. Both APKs current, gradle exit codes checked directly.

R1 — the pre-write refusal fired against a live session
Root of it: "the PROCESS has seen a newer bridge lifetime" and "THIS session predates the bridge answering" are different questions and the write path asked the first one. Each session is now bound to a bridge lifetime by the first beat that arrives ON it, and only a session whose bound lifetime differs from the one now answering is stale. New `SessionLifetime` class in app-phone\src\main\java\family\khaira\supersetcompanion\phone\data\SessionRehello.kt: `rebound()` on every negotiation, `observe(bridgeStartedMs)` returns stale, implemented in terms of the existing `bridgeRestarted` so the two comparisons cannot drift — one rule, two subjects. The repository keeps `lastBridgeStartedMs` for the process-level question (gseq drop / tree refetch), which is unchanged; `recordHeartbeat` now returns `BeatVerdict(bridgeRestarted, sessionStale)` and `heartbeatOnce` feeds the first to `heartbeatConfirmsCachedTree` while `write` keys the pre-send refusal on the second.

Binding at hello is not possible and I want this on the record: `HelloResponse` (core\...\protocol\Session.kt:81) carries `bridge: BridgeInfo` = appVersion/hostServiceVersion/forkTag/protocol and NO start time, and a restart of the same build changes none of them. So the first beat is the only place the binding can come from. Residual window: a restart between `hello` and the first beat on that session binds it to the lifetime that replaced its issuer — one sealed refusal, then `invalidatesCachedSession` re-hellos. It can never cost a duplicate write, since nothing is re-sent.

R2 — unsynchronized, unscoped invalidation
`wireSession` and its lifetime binding are now one fact behind one monitor (`sessionLock`, deliberately NOT `sessionMutex`: that one is a suspending mutex held across a whole `hello` round trip, and invalidation runs on failure paths that must not queue behind an in-flight negotiation). Every access is under it — session(), publishPairing, the tree-read gseq write at :497, recordHeartbeat.
Invalidation is identity-scoped via `discardSessionIf(origin)`, a compare-and-set on `wireSession !== origin` inside the lock. `read`, `write` and `probeLiveness` capture the session the call actually went out on and pass it to `handle(e, origin)`; `recordHeartbeat` only consults the ledger when `wireSession === origin`, so a beat in flight when the session rotated cannot condemn its replacement. `handle` takes origin with NO default — a caller that cannot name its session has to say so rather than silently getting null. Device-level failures (revoked, unknown_device/not_paired, re-pair, unpair) keep the unconditional `discardSession()`: nothing built on the old device key may survive, so scoping those would be wrong.

Tests — SessionRehelloTest.kt now 14 tests, all passing. The R1 sequence is driven end to end in `the retry after a restart is not refused`: beat on A → rebound (the 501 discard) → rebound (fresh hello) → assert the first-ever beat reporting B does NOT refuse → assert a later restart to C still does. Plus first-beat-binds, steady state across repeated beats, the original stale case, and that a rebound session does not inherit the discarded one's lifetime. On your "repository-level coverage" point: the Keystore-bound CompanionCore does block it, so I took the extraction route you named. The one part with no test is the identity check itself (`wireSession === origin` under the lock) — the test class says so explicitly rather than implying full coverage.

Gradle (exit codes read directly, no pipes)
- :app-phone:assembleDebug :app-phone:testDebugUnitTest :app-watch:assembleDebug → exit 0. 150 phone tests across 16 classes, failures="0" errors="0" in all 16 XMLs. Zero compiler warnings.
- :core:testDebugUnitTest filtered to the three real suites → exit 0, 42 tests, 0 failures. (CrossRuntimeDifferentialVectors still refuses under plain gradle by design; untouched.)
- app-watch:assembleDebug reported UP-TO-DATE this round, correctly: `git diff --stat HEAD` confirms a6dcd98 touches only app-phone (BridgeCompanionRepository.kt, SessionRehello.kt, SessionRehelloTest.kt), and the watch APK already contains 9b9aae2's core + watch changes.

APKs: C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\superset-companion\app-phone\build\outputs\apk\debug\app-phone-debug.apk (rebuilt) and ...\app-watch\build\outputs\apk\debug\app-watch-debug.apk (from 9b9aae2, still current).

# Adversarial review — af4994acd..HEAD (4 commits)

Verdict up front: **FIX-FIRST**. One MAJOR, six MINOR. No BLOCKER. The MAJOR is an interaction between two commits in this batch that neither commit's own reasoning covers.

## [MAJOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:2196

**Defect:** guard 4 now abstains on EVERY keystroke, including the ones whose guard 5 is the WEAK `same_prompt` form. The abstain's whole justification — "guard 5 has proved THIS question's picker is on screen right now with the pressed row where the capture says it is" — is only true for the STRONG `item_picker` form. On a `same_prompt` keystroke guard 5 proves nothing about a picker being up at all.

**Failure (executed):** I ran `matchPromptStillOnScreen` against a viewport with NO picker on it — just the user's own prompt echo plus `⏺ Thanks — running the canary now.` — and it returned `{ok:true, reason:"match", digitMapped:false}`. The prompt text stays in the normal buffer after the picker closes; their own `N2_REVIEW_VIEWPORT` fixture shows the full echo. So the weak form cannot distinguish "picker up" from "picker gone".

Now combine that with `(FREETEXT-N2-PROVEN)`, which makes the tail of a last-question free-text answer `[freetext_open, freetext_body, "\r", "\r"]` — three consecutive `same_prompt` keystrokes, one of them arbitrary user text. Sequence: the phone's answer is admitted, `freetext_open` passes strongly, then the picker goes away for any reason that does not immediately write a `tool_result` (an Esc-cancel at the desk, an agent-side abort). Guard 1 still reads "unanswered", guard 6's `.askq` marker is still there because PostToolUse never fired, guard 5 weak passes off the echo, and guard 4 — which previously refused, because any later hook event clears `lastEventType` off `PermissionRequest` — now abstains. The free-text body is typed into the composer and the two `\r`s submit it, so the user's picker answer becomes an unsolicited prompt to a running agent. Before this batch the same terminal state cost one keystroke (`\r` into a composer) and guard 4 refused it.

**Fix:** gate the abstain on the strong form as well as on `LOAD_BEARING_GUARDS`. `evaluateGuards` already has `input.expectation`; require `input.expectation.kind === "item_picker"` and let `permission_axis` refuse as before whenever guard 5 only ran `matchPromptStillOnScreen`. Every argument in the `ABSTAINING_GUARDS` doc-block is written about what the strong matcher proves, so this preserves the rationale exactly and costs nothing on the digit keystrokes the abstain was aimed at.

## [MINOR] ...\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:979

**Defect:** the "a hole wearing a clip's clothes" refusal is unreachable for an option press. `freeTextWindows` is only computed when the free-text row is itself the row being pressed (line 937, `input.requireOptionIndex === freeText.index`), so on every `select_digit`/`toggle_digit` keystroke it is `null` and the check is a no-op.

**Failure (executed):** item with 4 options + free-text at index 4; screen = question line, `1. Retire the duplicates`, its description, `5. Type something.` — i.e. the picker's LAST row is plainly visible while rows 2-4 are not, which cannot be a bottom clip. `matchPickerScreen(..., requireOptionIndex: 0)` returns `{ok:true, reason:"match"}`. It survives because `clipBelow`'s edge proof is `gapIsExplained(..., to: lines.length)`, which returns `true` unconditionally once `to - from <= SCREEN_ROW_ADJACENT_SLACK`, so a last visible row within 3 lines of the snapshot end gets the bottom-clip verdict for free and the free-text contradiction is never consulted. On a full-height viewport the gap rule does bite (I confirmed the same screen padded out is refused `row_gap_unexplained`), which is why this is MINOR — but the check does not do the job its comment claims.

## [MINOR] ...\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:2201

**Defect:** `GuardOutcome.abstained` is read by nothing outside `guard-abstain.test.ts`, while `passed` DOES list `permission_axis` (via `advance()` at line 2216) even though `evaluation.permission_axis` is `false`.

**Failure:** the durable `answer_attempts` ledger records `guardsPassed: [..., "permission_axis"]` alongside `guards.permission_axis: false` and no abstain marker. That ledger is the one place this feature keeps evidence about what was observed at write time; a forensic reader sees a self-contradictory row and cannot tell an abstain from a bug. The comment at line 2192 says the raw reading is kept "rather than being written up to `true`" — true of `evaluation`, false of `guardsPassed`.

## [MINOR] ...\packages\host-service\src\companion\answer.ts:268

**Defect:** `ABSTAINING_GUARDS` is not covered by `assertGuardClassification` (line 310), and the runtime condition is the literal `ABSTAINING_GUARDS.includes("permission_axis")`. The array is a working kill switch for removal and inert for addition — while the header at line 44 names `assertGuardClassification` as what keeps the "forgeable may never permit" invariant honest "with ONE listed exception", implying the list is enforced.

**Failure:** adding `"transcript"` or `"askq_marker"` to the array neither crashes at load nor changes behaviour. The next person to add a second abstaining guard will add the name and nothing will happen.

**Fix:** extend `assertGuardClassification` to require every member be classified `forgeable` and be evaluated after every `load_bearing` guard, and drive the branch off list membership rather than a hardcoded name.

If the slug directory alone is transiently absent — an antivirus/OneDrive hydration stall on that subtree, a worktree path being recreated — `fs.stat(transcriptPath)` is ENOENT under a stat-able root and the verdict is `gone`. `forget()` then clears the fence row irreversibly and the buzz for a genuinely blocked agent is lost, which is the one failure direction this file says it will not take.

**B — the premise does not hold.** The screen is not captured once. `answer.ts:3117-3131` takes a fresh snapshot and re-runs the entire guard stack before every single keystroke, with `awaitScreenAdvance` proving the previous byte was consumed and the snapshot deliberately re-taken rather than reused. A sequence cannot be committed against a picker that advanced past the expected question: each per-question keystroke carries `expect: {item_picker, itemIndex: i}` and is matched strongly. Separately, the free-text row's *absence* on an option press was already unchecked before this batch (line 937 predates it), so that is not a regression.

**C — two consecutive returns.** Grammar and byte checks are sound: exactly one submitting keystroke and it is last, the editor-closing return carries `submits:false` and `assertShapeGrammar` throws if it ever claims otherwise, groups are order-checked against question index, and the `question < 2` / `freeTextGroups === 0` checks stop `freetext_many` masquerading as another shape. The gap is not the set — it is that the set says nothing about which FORM of guard 5 ran. See the MAJOR.

for (capability in requiredAnswerCapabilities(request)) requireCapability(capability)
```
`core\...\transport\BridgeClient.kt:304`; `requireCapability` at `:399`. `granted` comes from the hello handshake (`BridgeClient.kt:113` sends `Capability.CLIENT_SUPPORTED`).

`QuestionResponse.requiredCapabilities` (`Question.kt:95`) is **defined but never called** — dead code, no gate.

`Capability.CLIENT_SUPPORTED` (`Enums.kt:260-275`) already includes both `ANSWER_MULTI_QUESTION` and `ANSWER_MULTISELECT`. Nothing to add.

### 4. `Answer.kt` model — can construct multiselect at index &gt; 0

`core\src\main\java\family\khaira\supersetcompanion\core\protocol\Answer.kt`
- `:186` `sealed interface AnswerItem { val questionIndex: Int }`
- `:192` `SelectAnswer`, `:204` `MultiSelectAnswer(questionIndex, optionIndexes)`, `:225` `FreeTextAnswer`
- Only constraint on index: `if (questionIndex &lt; 0) throw ProtocolException(...)` (`:210`). **No cap at 0.**
- `AnswerRequest.init` (`:260-267`) requires positional identity `item.questionIndex == position` — so `MultiSelectAnswer(questionIndex = 2, …)` at list position 2 is valid.
- `forQuestion` (`:295-334`) validates each item against its own `QuestionItem.multiSelect`; no rule forbidding multiselect when `questions.size &gt; 1`.

### 5. `BridgeCompanionRepository.submitAnswer`

`app-phone\...\phone\data\BridgeCompanionRepository.kt:567-604`
- `:581` `BridgeMapping.answerRequest(draft)` — the only client-side shape validation; it is the protocol `init` blocks above, caught as `ProtocolException` → `AnswerOutcome.Failed(BAD_REQUEST)` (`:586`).
- `:604` `session.answer(request, session.liveness())` — POST `/v1/answer`, one attempt, no retry.
- Mapping is a straight 1:1 per-choice fan-out (`BridgeMapping.kt:375-398`), preserving `questionIndex` for every kind. No shape gate.

### 6. Composer support in a multi-question flow — yes, fully

`QuestionSheet.kt:123-140` iterates **every** `detail.questions` item, and `QuestionBlock` (`:248-315`) branches on `item.multiSelect` independently per item. `ConversationViewModel.toggleOption(questionIndex, optionIndex)` (`:396-408`) is keyed by `questionIndex` into a `Map&lt;Int, AnswerChoice&gt;`, so per-question multi-select toggles inside a multi-question flow already work. Auto-submit correctly suppresses:
```kotlin
val requiresExplicitSubmit get() = detail.hasMultiSelect || choices.values.any { it is AnswerChoice.FreeText }
```
(`ConversationViewModel.kt:109-110`) — so a single-select tap on Q0 will **not** fire early when Q1 is multi-select (`chooseOption` guard at `:390`).

**Watch** (`app-watch\...\ui\WatchRoot.kt:97-106`, `QuestionScreen.kt:39,113`) has a working multi-question step machine + review screen, but only renders single-select `OptionRow`s and refuses any multi-select at `Answerability.kt:64`. Unchanged by this work.

### 7. `UnanswerableReason` UNKNOWN fallback — yes, three layers

- `core\...\protocol\Enums.kt:115-129` — `UNKNOWN("unknown")` member + `UnanswerableReasonSerializer` with `UnanswerableReason.UNKNOWN` as the unrecognized-wire-string fallback
- `app-phone\...\phone\model\Question.kt:26-42` — phone enum with `UNKNOWN` → `"This question can't be answered from here."`
- `BridgeMapping.kt:254-262` — exhaustive `when`, no wildcard needed
- `app-watch\...\Answerability.kt:55-58` — `else -&gt;` arm prints the raw string verbatim

### 8. `freeTextOption` — server-driven, never synthesized

- `QuestionSheet.kt:304-312` — `item.freeTextOption?.let { … }`; rendered only when non-null, and **only in the single-select branch** (a multi-select item's free-text slot is silently not rendered — pre-existing, unrelated to question count)
- `BridgeMapping.kt:238-240` — null-preserving passthrough
- `AnswerDraft.validationError` `:191` — `if (item.freeTextOption == null) return "question ${item.index} has no free-text slot"`
- `Answer.kt:322-331` — server index must equal `item.options.size`
- Watch never offers it: `WearProtocol.kt` `freeTextLabel`, rendered as a footnote at `QuestionScreen.kt:88-92`

PROTOCOL.md:1584 explicitly says `// may be empty`, so this is legal — pre-existing for 1-question multi-select, not new.

## Adversarial review: (ANSWER-GUARDLESS) — 498be7a43 + working tree

```
[MINOR] C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\packages\host-service\src\companion\http.ts:1263
Defect: Protocol-0 refusal gated on `session !== null &amp;&amp; session.protocolVersion === 0`; sessionless requests skip it entirely.
Failure: Sessionless envelope sealed under protocol byte 0 (UNNEGOTIATED_PROTOCOL_CANDIDATES tries [1,0]) from a paired device now reaches /v1/answer — capability:null + requireLiveSession early-return removed the gate that previously blocked it. Contradicts §6.1 invariant "protocol 0 is frozen forever and has NO write path at all". Requires valid device long-term keys → no privilege gain, invariant erosion only.
Fix: Gate on `opened.protocolVersion === 0` (available at http.ts:1300) instead of the session's, so sessionless protocol-0 writes refuse identically.
```

No other findings survive verification. Candidates traced and dismissed:
- Zero-write throw reclassified failed/retryable: safe — `DaemonClient.send` (DaemonClient.ts:401-407) throws only before `sock.write`; throw at index 0 = provably zero bytes.
- Confirmed-on-enqueue (socket buffered, daemon dies pre-flush): matches explicit contract "every encoded synchronous PTY write succeeds → immediately return confirmed."
- `recordOutcome` persist-failure swallow after confirmed: pre-existing, outside this diff.
- Codex/null-agent answerable: deliberate, test-asserted (question-freshness.test.ts:134-141); falls under removed binding guard — not recommending restoration per task rule.

**Contract verification (all source-level or emulated):**
- Sessionless/unnegotiated/no-biometric answer: capability:null routes + requireLiveSession early-return (http.ts:1021); `confirmedBiometric` optional-validated (answerSchema ~663, types.ts); messageSchema still requires it. session-expired.test.ts + answer-guardless.test.ts boundary tests pass.
- Renderer-hidden/display-off/locked: no dependency in `handleAnswer` dep graph — writeInput = daemon-socket RPC, memory stores, host.db ledger, audit file; zero renderer/Electron/Win-session APIs. Source-level proof only.

- `RETRACT_TTL_MS = 86_400_000` — `config.ts:251`. Frame's own expiry, not the question's (client checks `isExpired` before `k`).
- Trigger choke point: `question-store` `onSettled` → `sender.cancelPending(questionId)` — `index.ts:601-616` (one place every ending passes through; `reconcile` deliberately excluded, `index.ts:808`).
- `cancelPending` — `push.ts:2188-2197`; `retract` (awaited) — `push.ts:2199-2210`; `sendRetraction` — `push.ts:1897-1931`. Cancels in-flight original via `SendToken`, runs behind it on the same per-question chain.
- Sent-record retention `PUSH_SENT_RECORD_RETENTION_MS = 86_400_000` — `push.ts:194`; cap `PUSH_MAX_SENT_RECORDS = 4_096` — `push.ts:202`. No record → retraction silently no-ops (logged, `push.ts:1905`).

**Phone cancel call sites**:
- `routePush` `PushKind.RETRACT` → `retractNotification` + `tellWatchResolved` — `PushRouting.kt:58-62`.
- Surface impl — `CompanionMessagingService.kt:151-170`.
- `CompanionNotifications.retract(questionId)` → `manager.cancel(questionId, ID_QUESTION)` — `CompanionNotifications.kt:281-283`.
- Foreground sweep (best-effort backstop, PROTOCOL §13.3) — `CompanionNotifications.kt:293-303`; driven by `SessionViewModel.sweepNotifications()` — `SessionViewModel.kt:219-231`, called on foreground/refresh (`:181`, `:214`).

**Watch relay path**:
- Phone → watch `WearPaths.QUESTION_RESOLVED = "/companion/v1/question/resolved"` — `WearProtocol.kt:49`; payload `WatchQuestionResolved` — `WearProtocol.kt:331`.
- Send: `WatchLink.sendResolved` — `WatchLink.kt:63-64`; `WatchBridge.pushResolved` — `WatchBridge.kt:184`, `:445`.
- Watch receive → `onResolved` → `QuestionNotifier.cancel(this, info.questionId)` — `CompanionWearListenerService.kt:51, 71-76`.
- Also cancels on confirmed answer (`:78-83`), on notification tap (`MainActivity.kt:87`), on dismiss (`MainActivity.kt:234`).
- Sweep results are relayed to the watch too — `SessionViewModel.kt:227-231`.

## 2. Lifecycle `superseded` — host-side send suppression ONLY

- Field decl + doc — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:62-68` ("A `sending` alert cannot be un-sent, but it must not be RE-held on failure").
- Set by `supersede(hostTerminalId, reason)` — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:429-446`. `held` → deleted outright; `sending` → flagged `superseded: true`.
- Called from exactly one place: `record()` when `outcome === "progress" || "session-end"` — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:532-534`.
- Consumed only in `holdAfterFailure` — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:353-360`: drops a *failed* delivery instead of re-holding it.
- Initialized `false` at `mint` — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:470`.

**Verdict**: purely host-side. It suppresses an unsent/retryable alert. Once `sendLifecycleAlert` succeeds, nothing reaches phone or watch. Explicitly by design:
- `push.ts:500-511` — "THERE IS NO RETRACTION FOR THESE AND THAT IS DELIBERATE… 'this turn ended' … do not stop being true because the user later opened the app."
- PROTOCOL.md ~2183 — "Lifecycle alerts are not retracted (§13.3)… the alert simply expires or is dismissed."
- PROTOCOL.md ~2124 — "the wrist keeps its own notification and the phone cannot retract it".
- Push-kind sets are closed per version: v1 `["q","r"]`, v2 `["g","e"]` — `push.ts:303-306`. No v2 retract kind exists; adding one requires widening `PUSH_KINDS_BY_VERSION` (v2 key set has no `n`, so a v2 retract fits the existing `["v","k","i","w","x"]` shape unchanged).
- `ALERT_TTL_MS = 6h` — `lifecycle-alerts.ts:11`. That is the only thing that ends a delivered alert host-side.

## 3. Green-dot CLEAR — exists, renderer-only, forwarded NOWHERE

- `pane-map-hook.ts` derives `lifecycle_outcome` from hook events only — `pane-map-hook.ts:2388-2405`. Mapping: `SessionEnd`→`session-end`, `StopFailure`→`hold`/`failed`, `Failed`→`failed`, `Start`→`progress`, `Stop`/`SubagentStop`→`ready`. Fields attached at `:2417-2419`.
- Explicit comment at `pane-map-hook.ts:2380-2381`: **"Only hook-originated events carry it; administrative status clears never pass through this producer."**
- Host route: `notifications.ts:298-306` → `forwardCompanionLifecycle` → `companion-lifecycle-sink.ts:57-87` → `sink.record`. Only the 5 hook outcomes exist (`companion-lifecycle-sink.ts:9, 27`).
- Green CLEAR actually lives in renderer zustand:
  - `useClearActivePaneAttention` — `useClearActivePaneAttention.ts:35-46`. Fires when the user focuses a pane whose status is `review` → `markTerminalSeen(terminalId, binding.lastEventAt)`.
  - `markTerminalSeen` impl — `stores/v2-notifications/store.ts:250-276`. Bumps `terminalSeenAt` (monotonic) and drops the source's `review` entry.
  - Other callers: `useMarkWorkspaceTerminalsSeen` (sidebar "mark read") — `useV2NotificationStatus.ts:94-105`; resync — `resyncAgentStatus.ts:361`.
- Confirms `types.ts:524-529`: *"There is no `green` / 'ready for review' and there never will be: the review axis lives in renderer storage the bridge cannot reach."*

**No IPC/tRPC forwarding of the clear exists.** `markTerminalSeen` has zero call sites outside `src/renderer`. Nothing reaches `companion-lifecycle-sink`.

## 4. Notification identity — both surfaces are addressable

| Surface | Question | Lifecycle |
|---|---|---|
| Phone | tag = `questionId`, id = `ID_QUESTION` (`CompanionNotifications.kt:343`, cancel `:282`) | tag = `push.eventId` (= alertId), id = `ID_LIFECYCLE` = 1003 (`CompanionNotifications.kt:215, 383`) |
| Watch | `questionId.hashCode()` (`QuestionNotifier.kt:134`, cancel `:59-61`) | `"lifecycle:$eventId".hashCode()` (`LifecycleAlertPresentation.kt:81`, notify `LifecycleNotifier.kt:80, 120`) |

Yes — a later push could target and cancel a specific earlier one. The alertId is already the notification identity on both surfaces (PROTOCOL.md §13.4: "That same id is the notification's identity on the phone and on the watch"). `LifecycleNotifier` has **no** `cancel` function — only `notify` (`wear/notify/` grep: `QuestionNotifier.cancel` exists, `LifecycleNotifier` has none). `CompanionNotifications` has no `cancelLifecycle` (only `retract`, `cancelLostContact` at `:278`).

**Watch dismiss path**: no lifecycle-dismiss Wear path exists. `WearPaths` is closed — `WearProtocol.kt:41-78`; `LIFECYCLE` (`:77`) is one-way notify-only, and its doc explicitly forbids reusing `QUESTION_RESOLVED` for it (`:69-76`). Watch listener `when(event.path)` has no lifecycle-cancel branch — `CompanionWearListenerService.kt:46-57`.

## 5. FCM collapse keys / data-message cancellation

- `collapse_key` is **mandatory** and asserted `=== data.i` — `push.ts:419-423`, built at `push.ts:429-441`. Applies to v1 and v2 alike.
- Retraction reuses the question's collapse key so an undelivered original queued at FCM is *replaced* rather than delivered-then-cancelled — `push.ts:1108-1113`, PROTOCOL.md §13.3.
- Data-only, no `message.notification` block; envelope key set closed to `["token","android","data"]` and android to `["priority","ttl","collapse_key"]` — `push.ts:307-308`.
- No other cancellation pattern (no `apns`, no `webpush`, no FCM "delete notification" API use).
- Dedicated test file exists for the question path: `packages/host-service/src/companion/push-retract.test.ts`. No lifecycle-retraction test.

## Verdicts

| Surface | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **question-phone** | **EXISTS** | `k:"r"` push + `manager.cancel(questionId, ID_QUESTION)` + foreground sweep backstop |
| **question-watch** | **EXISTS** | phone relays `QUESTION_RESOLVED` → `QuestionNotifier.cancel`; plus tap/dismiss/answer-confirmed cancels |
| **lifecycle-phone** | **ABSENT** | `superseded` never leaves the host. Nothing cancels `(eventId, ID_LIFECYCLE)` |
| **lifecycle-watch** | **ABSENT** | No Wear path, no `LifecycleNotifier.cancel`. PROTOCOL explicitly says the phone cannot retract the wrist copy |

### Minimal-fit additions if you want lifecycle retraction

Each mirrors an existing pattern exactly; no new concepts.

**A. Host → phone (v2 retract kind)**
1. Add `"c"` to `PUSH_KINDS_BY_VERSION["2"]` — `push.ts:305`. v2's key set `["v","k","i","w","x"]` needs no change (no `n`), unlike v1's retract which had to carry `n:"0"`.
2. Add `buildLifecycleRetractPushData` beside `buildLifecyclePushData` (`push.ts:523`), stamping `x = now + RETRACT_TTL_MS` — same (RETRACT-TTL) reasoning as `buildRetractPushData` (`push.ts:481`), *not* the alert's original expiry.
3. Add `PushSender.sendLifecycleRetraction(alertId, workspaceId)` enqueued on `enqueueOrdered(alertId, …)` — the chain is already keyed by `string` for exactly this (`push.ts:1406-1411`). Same `collapse_key` (= alertId) then overtakes an undelivered original for free.
4. `lifecycle-alerts.ts`: keep a bounded `delivered` map (alertId → workspaceHandle, sentAtMs) mirroring `push.ts`'s `sent` map, and have `supersede` (`:429-446`) fire the retraction for `state === "sent"` entries rather than only deleting/flagging. This is where `superseded` stops being purely internal.

**B. Phone client**
5. `PushKind` + `routePush` gain a `LIFECYCLE_RETRACT` branch (`PushRouting.kt:57-68` shape) → `notifications.cancelLifecycle(eventId)` = `manager.cancel(eventId, ID_LIFECYCLE)` — one line beside `retract` at `CompanionNotifications.kt:281`.
6. Release the `SeenLifecycleEvents` claim for that id (`LifecycleRouting.kt:87` `gate.release`) so a genuinely later redelivery is not swallowed.

**C. Phone → watch**
7. New `WearPaths.LIFECYCLE_DISMISS = "/companion/v1/lifecycle/dismiss"` — `WearProtocol.kt:78`, payload `{eventId}` (mirrors `WatchQuestionRequest`, `:496`). Its own path for the reason stated at `WearProtocol.kt:69-76`.
8. `WatchLink.sendLifecycleDismiss` beside `:81`; `WatchBridge.pushLifecycleDismiss` beside `pushLifecycle`.
9. Watch: `when` branch (`CompanionWearListenerService.kt:54`) → new `LifecycleNotifier.cancel(context, eventId)` calling `NotificationManagerCompat.from(context).cancel(LifecycleAlertPresentation.notificationId(eventId))` — byte-for-byte the shape of `QuestionNotifier.cancel` (`:59-61`).

**D. The green-dot problem (item 3) is the real blocker for the useful case**

A/B/C give you retraction on *supersession* (a new work cycle started). They do **not** give you "user read the chat → cancel the ready alert", because that signal does not exist outside the renderer. Minimal fit:

- Add a renderer→main→host-service forward at `markTerminalSeen` (`store.ts:250`) or, more narrowly, at `useClearActivePaneAttention.ts:44` (the focus-driven clear only — the sidebar bulk "mark read" is a different intent).
- Route it through a **new** outcome, e.g. `"seen"`, in `companionLifecycleFields` (`companion-lifecycle-sink.ts:8-10`) and `CompanionLifecycleEvent.outcome` (`:27`). It must **not** go through `pane-map-hook.ts` — that producer is hook-only by design (`:2380`) and mints a fresh `producerEventId` per invocation; a renderer clear needs its own id or the sink's dedupe window (`alreadyApplied`, `lifecycle-alerts.ts:495`) will mis-handle it.
- In `record()` (`lifecycle-alerts.ts:525`), treat `"seen"` like `"progress"`: call `supersede(hostTerminalId, "seen")`, which with change A.4 now also retracts delivered alerts.

Note the design tension worth surfacing before building: `types.ts:524-529` and `push.ts:500-511` both argue the ready alert is a fact about a past instant that does not stop being true. "User opened the chat" is the one input that genuinely falsifies *"there is something waiting for you to review"* — so this is an argued change to a stated invariant, not a gap-fill.</result>

## Spec deviations

1. **v1/v2 "golden builder" test** — FINAL §7 asks for byte-identical v1/v2 builders. Per §3 both builders were *edited* to v3, so there is no v2 builder left to pin. I pinned instead: `buildRetractPushData` (v1 `r`) byte-for-byte, the v1 160-byte cap, and v1's refusal of a smuggled name. `lifecycle-push.test.ts`'s old v2-shape assertion was updated to v3 — that is the deliberate change, not drift.
2. **Per-message TTL** — derived from `data.k` inside `buildEnvelope` rather than passed as a parameter. Stronger: a caller cannot pair a retraction with the wrong TTL.
3. **`markLifecycleSeen` only retracts `g`** — reading a chat does not un-kill a failed agent, so `e` alerts stand. FINAL §4 says "derive the `g` id", so this matches, but it is an explicit behavioural choice worth your eye.
4. **`test-setup.ts` polyfill added** (out of the stated scope) — the v2 store persists to `window.sessionStorage`, which the desktop test setup never provided, so `store.test.ts` was failing wholesale before my change. Adding the polyfill fixes 10 pre-existing failures alongside my 4 new tests.
5. **Title precedence** — `useTabTitle.ts` does not exist at the path FINAL §3 cites. I used the real ladder (`resolveDashboardSidebarTabTitle` + `terminalRuntimeRegistry.getTitle`): tab rename &gt; pane rename &gt; live terminal title &gt; nothing. A tab that would only resolve to the word "Terminal" sends `""` rather than a placeholder.
6. **Live-title subscription** — `terminalRuntimeRegistry.onTitleChange` calls `getOrCreateEntry`, which *creates* a runtime entry. I subscribe only where `has(terminalId)` is already true; a terminal opened later re-runs the effect via the layout change.

## Per-finding

**1. BLOCKER — lone surrogates (`push.ts`)** — Added `hasLoneSurrogate`, a deliberate UTF-16 **index walk** mirroring `AlertContext.isRenderable` (`AlertContext.kt:113-135`) unit for unit. Checked on the **raw** value before stripping/truncation, and it degrades the **whole field to `""`** — not strips the unit — because Kotlin's `name()` refuses such a name outright. Stripping would have shipped a name the phone accepts while the two ends disagreed about its text. (`for...of` was the trap: it yields a lone surrogate as an ordinary code point, so the defect passed straight through.) Kotlin's vectors ported into `push-schema.test.ts` (all 6 lone-surrogate shapes + well-formed-pair control) and a cross-end assertion in `push-v3-golden.test.ts` (one bad field costs one field).

**2. MAJOR — alert-id mismatch** — Both ends:
- *Renderer*: both seen sites now read the **review entry's `occurredAt`** (the stamp of the event that set the review axis) before `markTerminalSeen` deletes it, and send that. `binding.lastEventAt` still drives the seen mark itself — unchanged.
- *Host*: `findLiveReadyAlert` scans the live map for this terminal's `g` alert **first**; the recomputed hash is now the restart-only fallback. My new test caught that matching only `sent` still let a **held** alert fall through to a blind broadcast, so it matches **any** state (`sent` preferred, else newest) — knowing anything about the terminal is enough to prove the hash is unnecessary; `retractById` then does the right thing per state.
- Three regression tests: the SessionStart-advances-`lastEventAt` scenario, no-blind-broadcast-while-held, and no cross-terminal mismatching.

**3. MAJOR — unreachable resync repair** — Hoisted the clearing branch's own predicate into `replayedReviewAlreadySeen` and drove the repair off it. The `preEntry !== undefined` guard was self-defeating exactly as reported (the user's own `markTerminalSeen` deletes the entry). Capped at `MAX_SEEN_REPAIRS_PER_RESYNC = 10` per epoch with `seenRepairsSent`/`seenRepairsSkipped` in the logged result; `!seedColdStart` retained.

**4. MAJOR — `accepted:false` treated as sent** — `sentHash` is now recorded **only** on `accepted === true`. Anything else schedules a bounded readiness retry (1s/2s/4s, max 3), and a reconnect resets the budget via `forgetAlertContextSyncsForHost`. `reportTerminalSeen` reads its result too and logs an id-only diagnostic when unconsumed (deliberately not retried — it's one moment's event, and the resync repair path owns re-offering it).

**5. MAJOR — split-tab title** — New `resolveTabTitle` resolves **once per tab** and hands the same title to every terminal in it. Precedence: tab rename &gt; title-pane rename &gt; title-pane's live terminal title (only when that pane *is* the terminal) &gt; nothing. A non-terminal active pane now names the tab ("Changes"), rather than borrowing a terminal's name for a pane nobody is on. Four tests, incl. the previously-wrong per-terminal divergence.

**6. MAJOR/MINOR — missing DB revalidation** — The `lifecycleSeen` wrapper in `index.ts` now runs the same `findTerminal().originWorkspaceId` check the registry does, dropping mismatches with an id-only log. Deliberately fails **closed** here (unlike the alert path's fail-open rules): the cost of dropping is a notification lingering; the cost of acting wrongly is retracting an alert for a chat nobody read.

**7. MINOR — effect churn** — Derived `workspaceIds` from `workspacesKey`'s first segment and keyed both effects on it, so `releaseAlertContextSync` runs on genuine unmount/membership change instead of every layout tick. Layouts come off a ref. Note: I avoided `biome-ignore` here — the lint was right both times (first that the map was a real dependency, then that the key had become unused), so the deps are now honest rather than suppressed.

**8. MINOR — binary test file** — `push-schema.test.ts:367` raw NUL/SOH replaced with `\u0000\u0001`. Verified zero remaining raw control bytes; the file is ripgrep-readable again.

**9. MINOR — stale comments** — `index.ts` armPush: "None of it goes to FCM" corrected (the ids are read at fire time to resolve names; the ids themselves still never cross the wire, only the resulting names, and `transcriptPath`/`toolUseId` stay local). `lifecycle-alerts.ts` `alreadyApplied`: rewritten for the outcome-instant id input — same-instant re-deliveries now collapse, but a re-stamped POST still mints two ids, which is what the producer id is for.

<result>All five fixed and verified. Changes remain uncommitted in the worktree.

## Per-item

**1. MAJOR — repair eligibility depended on a visibility-suppressed write** — Confirmed the mechanism at `statusTransitions.ts:101`: a turn-end on a **visible** target returns `clearSources` and `axes: null`, so the replayed source is never `review` exactly when the user is sitting on the pane. Eligibility is now two durable facts, neither read from the post-replay store:
- `alreadyReadThrough` — `seenAt &gt;= row.lastEventAt` (sessionStorage, survives everything);
- `wouldRaiseReview` — the **pure** `resolveV2AgentStatusTransition` asked with `targetVisible: false` and empty `statuses`, i.e. "would this row's last event raise a green if nothing suppressed it".

The clearing branch keeps reading the real store (it legitimately acts on actual state). `!seedColdStart` retained.

**2. MAJOR — stale readiness retry could overwrite newer context** — Added `state.desired` (always the newest snapshot, recorded *before* the hash guard). `scheduleReadinessRetry` **takes no snapshot argument** any more and reads `desired` at fire time, so a retry can only push the host toward current truth. Retries are cancelled when a newer snapshot is queued, when the desired snapshot already equals `sentHash`, on host change, on reconnect (`forgetAlertContextSyncsForHost`), and on release. Also closed the adjacent window the same A/B ordering exposed: `sentHash` is now recorded only if the completed send is still the current intent, so a newer snapshot queued mid-flight can't be suppressed by its predecessor's hash.

**3. MAJOR — layout changes stopped being observed** — Restored the layout-keyed effect: `syncAlertContexts()` **and** the title-listener subscriptions now run on `[workspacesById]`, so opening a 2nd/3rd tab moves `tabCount` and a newly opened terminal gets its listener. Register/release stays on the membership-only `[workspaceIds, hostUrl]`. This is cheap precisely because of the earlier fix — `releaseAlertContextSync` no longer runs on ticks, so the hash guard survives and a no-op tick costs one comparison. The now-unused `workspacesRef` was removed.

**4. MAJOR — unconditional blind `c` broadcast** — The manager records `startedAtMs` at construction. In `markLifecycleSeen`, when `findLiveReadyAlert` returns null **and** `startedAtMs &lt;= seenThroughAt`, it returns without sending: this process has held alert state since before that instant, `sent`/`retracted` rows are retained until TTL, and the lookup accepts any state — so an empty answer is *proof of absence*, not ignorance. Only a host started **after** `seenThroughAt` broadcasts blind. Not logged (this is the ordinary warm-reconnect path). The `evictOldest` caveat is recorded in the comment. Four tests: the proof branch (silent), the restart branch (still broadcasts, now with `seenThroughAt` before manager start), proof-branch-doesn't-swallow-a-real-retraction, and 25 warm re-offers producing zero traffic.

**5a. MINOR — starving batch + untrue comment** — Added a `REPAIR_COOLDOWN_MS` (30 min) skip-set keyed `${hostUrl}:${terminalId}`, pruned on write and bounded at 512. Recently-repaired rows are skipped **without** spending the cap, so each epoch advances to rows it has not covered — rows 11+ no longer starve. Counters split into `seenRepairsSent` / `seenRepairsSkipped` / `seenRepairsDeferred`, and the comment now states what the code actually does.

**5b. MINOR — wrong privacy rationale in the doc** — Both the header and `resolveTabTitle` now say a non-terminal pane names the tab **only through an explicit rename**, because derived pane names (file basename, browser host/page title, comment author) are free text the 2026-08-16 waiver does not cover; recorded in FINAL.md §8. Both blocks stop pointing readers at the sidebar's `getPaneTitle`. Behaviour unchanged. The pinning test now uses a file pane named `secret-project-plan.ts` so the assertion demonstrates the leak it prevents.

MAJOR — listener subscribed to a shadow registry entry** — Verified the exact mechanism in `terminal-runtime-registry.ts`: `onTitleChange` → `getOrCreateEntry(terminalId, instanceId = terminalId)` mints the `(terminalId, terminalId)` default, and `getPrimaryEntry` then *prefers* that empty default — so `getTitle` starts answering `undefined` for everyone, not just the listener. Fixes:
- New non-creating `terminalRuntimeRegistry.hasInstance(terminalId, instanceId)`.
- New `collectTerminalPaneRefs(layout)` → `(terminalId, paneId)` pairs.

**2. MAJOR — equal-hash fast path ignored an older in-flight send** — The fast path now requires a *quiet* workspace (`pending === null &amp;&amp; timer === null &amp;&amp; inFlight === 0`), and every accepted completion reconciles: if its hash is not the current `desired`, it clears `sentHash` **and** enqueues `desired` rather than leaving nobody to push it. `inFlight` is tracked around the chained mutation. The A/C inversion is a regression test that fails against the old code (C early-returns, A lands, nothing re-queues C).

**3. MAJOR — eviction vs proof-of-absence** — (a) `unexpiredEvictionAtMs` latches whenever an *unexpired* row of any state is evicted, and proof-of-absence now requires `live === null &amp;&amp; startedAtMs &lt;= seenThroughAt &amp;&amp; unexpiredEvictionAtMs === null`. Coarse and global, as you allowed — at 512 live alerts a cheap wrong answer (a blind `c` the phone drops) beats an abandoned real notification. (b) `evictOldest` now logs **every** unexpired eviction loudly with the alert id and state; the "sent/retracted is only a tombstone and costs nothing" comment is gone (a `sent` row is the only record that can retract a live notification; a `retracted` row is the re-mint guard), and the "HONEST LIMIT" paragraph in `markLifecycleSeen` is rewritten since the limit no longer exists. Tests: evicted-sent, sending-evicted-then-accepted, and proof-disabled-after-eviction.

**4. MAJOR — cooldown recorded before the answer** — `reportTerminalSeen` now returns `Promise&lt;boolean&gt;` (never rejects). The repair records the attempt only once resolved: accepted → 30 min, `accepted:false` or transport failure → 60 s. Counters are `seenRepairsSent`/`Skipped`/`Deferred`.

**5. MINOR — unbounded cooldown map** — After age pruning, the map is trimmed **oldest-first** to `MAX_REPAIR_COOLDOWN_ENTRIES` (512), so 513 fresh entries can no longer sit above the bound.

**6. MINOR — `statuses: {}` hid `wasAwaitingPermission`** — When `row.pendingPermission === true` a synthetic `permission` entry is passed, so a turn-end that landed on a pending permission is correctly *not* eligible. `pendingPermission === null` (host could not read its markers) stays in the wider set, documented honestly and contained by #3's gating.

## The finding

Confirmed both halves before fixing:
- `layout.tsx:292` — `V2NotificationController` is an **earlier sibling** of `Outlet`, so its effect runs first on the commit that adds a pane.
- `TerminalPane.tsx:153-163` — the runtime is created in the pane's own effect via `terminalRuntimeRegistry.mount(terminalId, container, appearance, terminalInstanceId)`, which changes no layout state.

So `hasInstance(terminalId, paneId)` was correctly false at reconciliation, the pair was skipped, and nothing ever brought it back. The pane-key fix made the *check* correct but left the *lifecycle* one-shot.

## Fix

**`terminal-runtime-registry.ts`** — added `onInstanceRegistered(cb)`, a read-only observer notified from `getOrCreateEntry` **after** the entry is fully registered (so an observer calling `hasInstance`/`onTitleChange` for that pair sees it). Listener throws are caught and logged — an observer must never break terminal creation. The no-default-entry guarantee is untouched: this path creates nothing, and `hasInstance` remains the only existence check.

**`terminalTitleListeners.ts`** — reconciliation now keeps a **waiting list** of exact pairs whose runtime did not exist. When the registry announces one, it installs the title listener against that exact `(terminalId, paneId)` and fires `onTitleChange()` once — the runtime that just registered may already carry a title the reconciliation pass could not read, which is what makes the context sync fire. The instance observer is only subscribed when something is actually pending, and teardown clears the waiting list plus every listener, including ones installed late.

## Count reconciliation

Sol was right — the file had **9** tests, not 10; my previous report miscounted. It now has **17** (9 original + 8 new), and the totals below are recomputed from actual runs rather than carried forward.

New tests in the `pending instances` block:
- **the exact sequence**: pair absent at reconciliation → `registerInstance` with *no* layout change → exact `(terminal-1, pane-1)` listener installed → sync fired once;
- title changes forwarded through a late-installed listener;
- a non-pending instance (different terminal, and same terminal under a different pane) installs nothing;
- each pending pair subscribes exactly once across a remount;
- present pairs subscribe immediately while only missing ones wait;
- the instance observer exists only when something is pending;
- teardown before the instance arrives installs nothing;
- teardown after a late install still unsubscribes it.

Also fixed two more literal NUL bytes that had been written into `terminalTitleListeners.ts` and its test (same ripgrep-binary hazard as round 2's `push-schema.test.ts`) — both now use `\u0000`.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Fixed and verified. Changes remain uncommitted in the worktree.

## The finding

Confirmed all three legs before fixing:
- `terminal-runtime-registry.ts:196` `deleteEntry` removes the entry; `disposeEntry` calls it after `disposeTransport`.
- `terminal-ws-transport.ts:1016` `disposeTransport` does `transport.titleListeners.clear()` — so the old teardown is inert and a title event reaches nothing.
- `getOrCreateEntry` returns early for an existing entry, so recreation genuinely requires delete-then-create, and only that announces.

My previous version tracked only pairs missing at *initial* reconciliation, so an initially-present pair left `pending` empty — and with every pair present, no `onInstanceRegistered` observer was armed at all. After the parked-runtime cap disposed a runtime, re-opening the workspace recreated and announced the pair with nobody listening. No layout change would ever come.

## Fix

`subscribeTerminalTitleListeners` now keeps the **full desired set** and an instance-registration observer for the whole subscription lifetime:
- `desired: Map&lt;pairKey, TerminalPaneRef&gt;` — every pair the layouts want, whether or not its runtime exists.
- `titleUnsubscribes: Map&lt;pairKey, () =&gt; void&gt;` — the *current* teardown per pair.
- `install(ref)` calls the recorded teardown first, then subscribes against the exact `(terminalId, paneId)` — so a re-announcement **replaces** rather than stacks (and after a dispose the stale teardown is harmlessly inert).
- The observer is armed whenever `desired` is non-empty, not only when something is missing; any announcement for a desired pair re-installs and fires the sync.
- Teardown stops the observer, runs every recorded teardown, and clears both maps.

The no-default-entry guarantee is unchanged: `hasInstance` is still the only existence check and nothing on this path can create an entry.

## Test double corrected

The fake registry now models the real lifecycle rather than a convenient one:
- `registerInstance` announces **only on real creation**; an existing pair returns early and announces nothing (matching `getOrCreateEntry`).
- `disposeInstance` deletes the key **and clears that pair's live listeners** (matching `disposeEntry` + `disposeTransport`).
- `fireTitle` reaches only listeners attached to the runtime live *right now* — so "the listener was reinstalled" is a behavioural assertion, not bookkeeping.

That also retired the misleading "remount subscribes once" test, which had announced a still-existing pair twice; it is now an explicit assertion that re-announcing a live pair is a no-op.

## New tests (`disposed and recreated runtimes`)

- **the required regression**: present initially → subscribed and live (`fireTitle` syncs) → `disposeInstance` (`fireTitle` now syncs nothing) → exact recreation **with no layout change** → listener reinstalled, sync fired, and the new listener verified live;
- several dispose/recreate cycles leak nothing (each install replaces; exactly one live at teardown);
- only the recreated pair is re-subscribed, not its neighbours;
- a recreation of a pair the layouts never wanted is ignored;
- after teardown, recreation installs nothing.

Plus the observer-arming assertion was inverted to match the fix: it is now armed whenever anything is desired (present or not), and only skipped when the layouts want no terminals at all.

- **Biome** (lint + format) across all touched paths: **0 diagnostics**.
- **Marker gate** (bash): **All 160 present.**

No other behaviour was touched; the registry's `onInstanceRegistered` and `hasInstance` additions are unchanged from the previous round.</result>

<summary>Agent "Simplify review: efficiency angle" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>All diffs gathered and read (fork worktree host-service + renderer incl. new untracked files; companion Kotlin core/phone/watch + PROTOCOL.md). Efficiency-only findings:

## Findings

**1. Renderer: full title-listener teardown/rebuild + full re-extraction on every layout tick**
- File: `C:\Users\khair\Code Projects\fork-superset-windows-arm64\.claude\worktrees\agent-a4b4acd04792ec9a3\apps\desktop\src\renderer\routes\_authenticated\components\V2NotificationController\components\HostNotificationSubscriber\HostNotificationSubscriber.tsx`, lines 324–335.
- Summary: effect keyed on `workspacesById` — which its own sibling comment (line 280) documents as "rebuilt on every layout live-query tick, several times a second". Each run tears down and re-installs a title listener for EVERY `(terminalId, paneId)` pair across all workspaces (`subscribeTerminalTitleListeners` full rebuild: two Maps, `collectTerminalPaneRefs` walk, unsubscribe+resubscribe per pair, re-arm `onInstanceRegistered`), plus runs `syncAlertContexts()` = `extractAlertContexts` + `alertContextsHash` JSON.stringify for every hydrated workspace. The in-code claim "a tick that changed nothing costs one hash comparison" undercounts: it costs full listener churn + N extractions + N stringifys before the hash guard is consulted.
- Cost: O(workspaces × panes) allocations + listener set mutations, several times/sec, continuously, in the renderer.
- Cheaper: split the effect. (a) Key listener subscription on a stable fingerprint of the pane-ref set (`collectTerminalPaneRefs` pair keys joined, via `useMemo`) so churn happens only when the set changes — same pattern already used for `workspaceIds`/`workspacesKey`. (b) For per-tick sync, skip workspaces whose `paneLayout` reference is unchanged (cache last ref per workspace) so one workspace's tick doesn't re-extract all of them. Semantics (sync on layout change, listeners for late-arriving panes) preserved.

**2. Renderer: extraction of ALL workspaces per terminal title change, upstream of the debounce**
- File: same `HostNotificationSubscriber.tsx` lines 169–186 + `...\lib\companionAlertSync.ts` line 163 (debounce sits inside `queueAlertContextSync`, after extraction).
- Summary: `onTitleChange` → `syncAlertContexts()` extracts + hashes every hydrated workspace on each title event; `alertContexts.ts` line 277 itself notes "several a second". The 250 ms debounce only suppresses the mutation, not the extraction/stringify burst.
- Cost: N-workspace layout walks + JSON.stringify per prompt-redraw title event.
- Cheaper: debounce first — one trailing 250 ms timer around `syncAlertContexts` itself (extraction at fire time is also fresher), and/or have `subscribeTerminalTitleListeners` pass the changed `terminalId` so only owning workspaces re-extract (the desired-pair map already knows the mapping).

**3. Renderer: `wouldRaiseReview` + synthetic statuses computed for every row, used only on the repair branch**
- File: `...\lib\resyncAgentStatus.ts` (diff hunk @ line ~490; `wouldRaiseReview` / `syntheticStatuses` construction).
- Summary: `resolveV2AgentStatusTransition(...)` and the `syntheticStatuses` object are built unconditionally per snapshot row; results consumed only inside `if (alreadyReadThrough &amp;&amp; wouldRaiseReview &amp;&amp; !seedColdStart)`.
- Cost: one resolver call + object allocation per terminal per resync (all terminals across all workspaces), wasted whenever `alreadyReadThrough` is false or `seedColdStart` — the majority of rows.
- Cheaper: compute lazily under `alreadyReadThrough &amp;&amp; !seedColdStart`. Pure reorder, no semantic change.

**4. Host: `listProjects().find()` full-table read per alert send**
- File: `...\packages\host-service\src\companion\index.ts` line ~720 (`resolveAlertContext`).
- Summary: resolving one project name loads every project row (`projectsStmt.all()`) then linear-scans; runs per question push, per lifecycle alert send attempt (each retry re-resolves — that freshness is deliberate, the scan isn't).
- Cost: full `projects` materialization + scan per push.
- Cheaper: prepared `SELECT ... FROM projects WHERE id = ?` (`findProject`) alongside the existing `findWorkspace`/`findTerminal` point statements in `read-api.ts`.

**5. Host: N point queries per accepted tab-context snapshot**
- File: `...\packages\host-service\src\companion\push-context.ts` lines 238–248 (`sync`).
- Summary: `deps.db.findTerminal(terminalId)` per terminal to validate placement — up to 256 prepared-statement round trips per snapshot; snapshots arrive per debounced title burst per workspace.
- Cost: N SQLite point lookups per sync mutation instead of 1 query.
- Cheaper: one `SELECT id FROM terminal_sessions WHERE origin_workspace_id = ?` (or `id IN (...)`) then Set membership; same rejection semantics.

**6. Renderer: snapshot hash recomputed up to 3× per sync cycle**
- File: `...\lib\companionAlertSync.ts` lines 153, 168, 190.
- Summary: `alertContextsHash` (JSON.stringify) computed in the fast path, again in the timer callback, again for `desiredHash` — same or overlapping snapshots.
- Cost: redundant stringify per debounce cycle (per workspace).
- Cheaper: compute once per snapshot at `queueAlertContextSync` entry and carry `{snapshot, hash}` through `desired`/`pending`.

**Trivial (noting, not pressing):** `sanitizePushName` (`push.ts`) runs a regex test per code point; a single global-flag `.replace()` does the strip in one pass — ≤3 names ≤256 bytes per send, so negligible. `assertPushDataSafe` sorts `Object.keys(keysByKind)` per call though it's only needed for the error message. `companionAlertSync.ts` lines 320–345 carry a fully duplicated JSDoc block (dead text, not runtime cost).

## Clean areas
- No blocking fs/sync work added to desktop main-thread startup (renderer/host-service only; test-setup is test-only). The repo's startup footgun rule is not violated.
- Closures: `resolveAlertContext`, sinks, and `HeldAlert` records copy scalar fields; no long-lived closure captures a large scope.
- Companion (Kotlin): clean on this angle. `SeenLifecycleEvents.suppress` / `PendingLifecycleDismissals` `commit()` writes are per-retraction (rare, user-action-rate), documented as durability-required on the FCM thread, and write-before-send ordering is a PROTOCOL §13.5.5 ruling — the common-case double write of the pending file (enqueue then clear) is the design, not waste. `enqueue` already skips the write when the id is present and nothing expired. No repeated JSON serialization introduced (the `encodedSize`/send double-encode predates this diff). Watch/phone composition is a handful of small string builds per push.

Frozen wire schema, golden vectors, and FINAL.md §8 rulings untouched by every suggestion above — all six findings are implementation-cost changes with identical observable behavior.</result>
