## Incident during this task — recovered

While applying item 18 I used PowerShell `Set-Content -NoNewline` on a line array, which concatenated `lifecycle-alerts.ts` into a single 37 KB line. No Entire checkpoints existed on this branch.

I recovered it rather than losing the work: the damaged file is exactly `lines.join("")`, so I reconstructed newlines with a dictionary of the file's last committed lines (byte-exact for unedited regions) plus heuristics for lines added this session, then re-indented block-comment interiors and restored blank lines. Every pass was verified by re-joining the result and byte-comparing against the damaged file, so nothing could be invented or lost. Final state: **345/345 tests pass, tsc clean, biome clean, diff vs HEAD is 454 added / 27 removed with zero blank-line loss.** Recovery scripts were deleted. I did not use that PowerShell idiom again.

## Skipped (1) — reverted per the rules

**Item 8** (wrap `resolveContextSafely` once at the composition root, delete both consumer wrappers). `push-armed-context.test.ts` → *"still fires when the resolver THROWS"* passes a throwing resolver **directly into `createPushSender`** and asserts the entry still lands in `sent`. That is real behavior — a broken resolver must cost the name, never the buzz — and item 8 would require deleting or weakening it, since `resolveAlertContext` is an injection point any caller supplies. I reverted all three edits (push.ts, lifecycle-alerts.ts, index.ts) and left a comment at the push.ts guard explaining why it stays.

Two consequential notes on applied items:
- **#9** required the lifecycle-seen sink to return `boolean` (the slot's `call` returns the sink's value). I made it return `true` on **every** path — including the DB-revalidation drops — so `accepted` keeps meaning "a running bridge received it", exactly as before. Returning `false` there would have changed behavior: the resync would classify a permanent drop as a transient failure and retry it on the short cooldown.
- **#14**'s test seam had to change shape: the map now stores a deadline, and a deadline minus a clock the test doesn't share isn't a duration. `__peekRepairCooldownForTest` became `__peekRepairOutcomeForTest` returning `"repaired" | "failed" | null` — the distinction the branch actually makes, asserted exactly rather than approximately. No test was weakened.

## `(ONE-BUZZ-UNTIL-READ)` — implemented

**1. Reason threaded through the state machine.** Replaced the free-form `reason: string` with an explicit type, so the one silent case can't be reached by accident:

```ts
type RetireReason = { kind: "new-cycle" } | { kind: "session-end" } | { kind: "seen" };
```

The whole policy is one predicate:

```ts
function notifiesPhone(alert, reason) {
  return !(alert.kind === "g" &amp;&amp; reason.kind === "new-cycle");
}
```

- `record()` now splits what it previously merged: `progress` → `{kind:"new-cycle"}`, `session-end` → `{kind:"session-end"}` (both still supersede; only the notification differs).
- `retireAlert`: `sent` `g` + new-cycle → transitions to `retracted` **without** `fireRetraction`, keeping the row as the cycle's dedupe record and leaving the notification standing. Logs the silent retirement rather than going quiet in the diagnostics too.
- `markDelivered` (superseded mid-flight): `HeldAlert` gained `supersededReason`, recorded when the flag is set, because by the time the send lands the event that superseded it is gone. Same predicate decides. A missing reason defaults to **fire** — a spare `c` the phone drops is cheaper than a notification for a chat that no longer exists.
- `markLifecycleSeen` → `{kind:"seen"}` → always fires, so reading resets the cycle and the next finish buzzes fresh.
- `held` → deleted, never fires (nothing was ever delivered). `retracted` → inert, storm guard unchanged.
- **`e` untouched**: excluded by the predicate, so an unseen error is never quietly folded away.

**3.** No wire change — v3 `g`/`c` already carry `t`. No renderer change.

## Tests (lifecycle-alerts 36 → 40)

Rewritten: *"retracts a SENT alert when the terminal moves on"* → **does NOT retract on a new cycle** (storm guard assertion retained, now asserting zero retractions across 20 progress events); *"does NOT re-mint after retracted"* → same dedupe guarantee, now proving the silent path still **transitions state rather than deleting the row**; *"retracts an alert superseded MID-FLIGHT"* → split into the new-cycle (stays standing) and session-end (comes down) variants.

New: **session-end retracts a delivered ready alert**; **the next finish still buzzes fresh** after a silent retirement (two distinct alert ids, zero retractions); **a delivered ERROR alert still retracts on a new cycle** — pinning the `e` boundary the rule deliberately excludes.

Unchanged and still green: seen-retracts, seen idempotence, proof-of-absence, eviction, restart fallback.

One thing to confirm on the companion side before install: this relies on the phone keying `g` notifications **by terminal handle** so the next alert replaces the standing one in place. If that keying isn't in the build you install, the silent retirement would leave the old notification stacked rather than replaced — the opposite of the intent.</result>

<result>## Verdicts

**Q1 — Sessions X: it DOES route through the soft-delete, but the item then falls into a void.**

- X button: `apps/desktop/src/renderer/routes/_authenticated/_dashboard/components/DashboardSidebar/components/DashboardSidebarWorkspaceItem/components/DashboardSidebarExpandedWorkspaceRow/DashboardSidebarExpandedWorkspaceRow.tsx:442-471` (`aria-label="Close workspace"`, `HiMiniXMark`, → `onCloseWorkspaceClick()` at :448).
- Wiring: `DashboardSidebarWorkspaceItem.tsx:367-373` — `sectionState === "deleted" ? handleDeletePermanently : handleDelete`. A session row has `sectionState === undefined` → `handleDelete`.
- Handler: `useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:194-196` → `deleteWorkspace(workspaceId, projectId)` with `projectId === null` for sessions.
- Soft-delete: `useDashboardSidebarState.ts:759-829` — sets `sidebarState.deletedAt = Date.now()`, `isHidden = true`, clears archive/snooze/completed. Lossless; no worktree/branch touched.

So it soft-deletes correctly. The break is downstream: `useDashboardSidebarData.ts:410-458` buckets it into `deletedSidebarWorkspaces`, and the ONLY consumer is the project-tree loop at `:898-904` (`projectsById.get(workspace.projectId)` → `continue` on null). Snoozed/archived get a session partition (`:704`, `:710`); **deleted does not**. Net effect: X on a session = row disappears permanently with no bin, no restore, no trace in UI.

Two latent defects worth noting:
- `deleteWorkspace(workspaceId, projectId?: string)` (`:760`) is called with `string | null` from two sites (`useDashboardSidebarWorkspaceItemActions.ts:195`, `V2WorkspaceRow.tsx:158`) under `strict: true` (`tooling/typescript/base.json:18`) — accepted type debt.
- No-local-state-row fallback (`:787-798`) refuses when `resolvedProjectId` is falsy → a session with no local row silently console.errors. In practice sessions always have a local row (`isAutoIncludedLocalMainWorkspace` requires `projectId !== null`, `sidebarVisibility.ts:46`), so the update path runs.

**Q2 — Recycle Bin implementation.** Marker: `(RECYCLE-BIN)`, `FEATURES.md:72`.

- 30-day window: `recycleBinRetention/recycleBinRetention.ts:4` (`DEFAULT_RECYCLE_BIN_RETENTION_DAYS = 30`), clamp `:22-30`, store `:46-68` (zustand persist, key `recycle-bin-retention`). Filter fn: `sidebarVisibility.ts:186-193` (`isWithinRecycleBinWindow`). Display-only — nothing auto-purges. Applied in `DashboardSidebarStateSection.tsx:106-117` and `useKanbanData.ts:671-677`.
- Soft-delete entry points today: sidebar row X (above); sidebar row context menu Delete (`DashboardSidebarWorkspaceItem.tsx:297-301`, item at `DashboardSidebarWorkspaceContextMenu.tsx:424-431`); command palette "Delete &lt;ws&gt;" + `CLOSE_WORKSPACE` hotkey (`commandPalette/modules/workspace/commands.tsx:74-84` → `ui/DeleteWorkspaceMount/DeleteWorkspaceMount.tsx:38`); kanban card Delete (`KanbanCard.tsx:345,416` → `useKanbanActions.ts:617`); workspaces list row (`V2WorkspaceRow.tsx:157-159`); dead-worktree page (`WorkspaceMissingWorktreeState.tsx:44-47`). **Exception:** multi-select bulk "Delete N Workspaces" (`DashboardSidebarWorkspaceBulkContextMenu.tsx:109-113` → `useBulkWorkspaceDelete/bulkWorkspaceDelete.ts:25-51`) is a HARD git destroy, not a bin move.

## Per item

**A1 — deleted bucket partitioned** (`useDashboardSidebarData.ts:716-724, :909, :1017, :1065-1080, :1100`). `deletedSidebarWorkspaces` now splits via `partitionSidebarWorkspacesBySession`; project rows feed the project-tree loop (`projectDeletedRows`), session rows build `deletedSessionWorkspaces` (variant `"deleted"`), returned from the hook and threaded through `DashboardSidebar.tsx`.

**A2 — builder** (`buildDashboardSidebarProjects.ts`). `SidebarInactiveWorkspaceInput.deletedAt: number | null`; `SidebarSessionLifecycleVariant` widened to `"deleted"`; `deletedAt` passed through the decorated row; sort is now a 3-way (snoozed→wake asc, archived→archivedAt desc, deleted→deletedAt desc).

**A3 — prefs schema** (`dashboardSidebarLocal/schema.ts`). `showDeletedSessions` / `deletedSessionsCollapsed` added to `v2UserPreferencesSchema`, `DEFAULT_V2_USER_PREFERENCES`, and `SESSION_SECTION_FLAGS` (so the boolean read-heal covers them). Both default false = hidden, expanded when revealed — identical to the project flags.

**A4 — sessions lane** (`DashboardSidebarSessionsSection.tsx`). Third `stateSections` entry: variant `"deleted"`, title `Recycle Bin`, Restore-all loops `restoreWorkspace`. Verified the null-projectId paths flow: per-row Restore (`handleRestore` → `restoreWorkspace(id)`) and context-menu Delete permanently (`useDestroyWorkspaceIntent` → `DestroyWorkspaceMount` → `DashboardSidebarDeleteDialog`) are both projectId-free, and the retention/"Show all" filter in `DashboardSidebarStateSection` already keys off `workspace.deletedAt`. **Not implemented:** section-level "Empty Recycle Bin" for sessions (`onEmptyBin` is optional; the project version duplicates host-destroy plumbing in `useDashboardSidebarProjectSectionActions`). Scope only required per-row destroy — flagging as a deliberate gap.

**A5 — toggle** (`DashboardSidebarSessionsContextMenu.tsx`): third item, `LuTrash2`, "Show/Hide Recycle Bin". No footer button, no palette command.

**A6 — type debt** (`useDashboardSidebarState.ts:761-800`): `deleteWorkspace(id, projectId?: string | null)`; the host record is resolved once, and the insert path uses the new pure `resolveSoftDeleteProjectId(explicit, hostProjectId)` (in `sidebarMutations.ts`) so an explicit null is honoured instead of hitting the old "projectId unresolvable" refusal. That refusal branch is now unreachable and removed (the record is guaranteed by the type guard above).

**B — bulk delete → soft delete.** Both bulk entry points (context menu `DashboardSidebarWorkspaceBulkContextMenu` **and** the selection toolbar `DashboardSidebarBulkActions`, which shared the same dialog) now call the new `useBulkWorkspaceSoftDelete` → pure `executeBulkWorkspaceSoftDelete`, which loops the same `deleteWorkspace` used by single rows; mains are skipped and stay selected. **Deviation to review:** with no caller left, I deleted the dead hard-destroy stack — `components/DashboardSidebarBulkDeleteDialog/**` (dialog, failures pane, `useBulkWorkspaceDelete`, `bulkWorkspaceDelete.ts` + its test) and `hooks/useBulkWorkspaceDeleteDialog/**`. Keeping the dialog would have lied ("removes every selected worktree from disk", uncommitted-work inspection, force retries) about a lossless soft delete. In-bin rows are not bulk-selectable (state sections pass no `onSelectionClick`), so no permanent-destroy capability is reachable-but-lost beyond bulk destroy itself.

<result>Round 4 MAJOR fixed — the proof epoch is now on the same per-terminal logical timeline as the generations it reasons about.

## The fix (`lifecycle-alerts.ts`)

New required dep `proofEpochs: (() =&gt; Iterable&lt;{hostTerminalId, lastEventAtMs}&gt;) | null`, read **once** at construction into `proofEpochByTerminal`. Proof-of-absence moved out of the inline condition into one predicate:

```
canProveAbsence(terminalId, seenThroughAt) =
  !proofDisabled
  &amp;&amp; !hasEvictedUnexpired
  &amp;&amp; startedAtMs &lt;= seenThroughAt
  &amp;&amp; (epoch === undefined ? !clockIsBehindPersistedTimeline
                          : seenThroughAt &gt; epoch)
```

- **Per-terminal epoch.** With monotonic instants this process's first stamp for a terminal is `epoch + 1` at the earliest, so `seenThroughAt &lt;= epoch` means the generation belongs to a **predecessor** — its row died with that process, silence is ignorance, blind `c`. Sol's scenario (start 4000, persisted A at 5000, read at 5000) now fails `5000 &gt; 5000` and broadcasts.
- **`clockIsBehindPersistedTimeline`** — any persisted instant `&gt;= startedAtMs` is the backstep signature, so terminals with *no* epoch row (nothing to compare against) also lose the proof while that holds. Without it the same false proof leaks through any terminal whose binding row didn't survive. In the ordinary restart (all persisted instants below start) this is `false` and nothing changes — the blind-broadcast guard is untouched.
- **Failure is conservative, not fatal.** `null` (no restart evidence) disables proof outright; a throwing epoch read logs `could not read the lifecycle proof epoch…` and disables it, rather than killing bridge start. A blind `c` the phone drops is the safe failure; a swallowed retraction is not.

Composition root (`companion/index.ts`) supplies it from `hostDb.listBindings()` — which has no `ended_at` filter, so ended sessions' `last_event_at` counts too.

## Tests (`lifecycle-alerts.test.ts`, new describe, 7 added → 57 pass)

- **Required case**: restart at 4000 / persisted A at 5000 / read at 5000 → blind `c` with `alertId = readyAlertIdFor(5_000)` and `gx = 5_000`.
- **Held-5001 variant**: same restart, this process mints 5001 (held, present), read at 5000 → the newer row is filtered out and the blind `c` for 5000 still fires.
- Proof is still claimed for `seenThroughAt = 5_001` (a generation only this process could have minted).
- One terminal's epoch cannot silence another terminal's read (per-terminal, not a global floor).
- Unknown-terminal behaviour both ways: timeline ahead of the clock → blind; timeline behind (ordinary restart) → silence preserved.
- `proofEpochs: null` → no proof; epoch read throws → logged loudly and no proof.

`setup()` gained `startAtMs`, `proofEpochs`, `epochThrows` seams; every existing test defaults to an empty snapshot, i.e. unchanged behaviour.

<result>## 1. Current pairing, end to end

**QR payload** — built at `packages/host-service/src/companion/pairing.ts:984-986`:
```
superset-companion://pair#v=1&amp;h=&lt;lanIP:47611&gt;&amp;pid=&lt;22&gt;&amp;pc=&lt;43&gt;&amp;fp=&lt;22&gt;
```
Fields normative in `PROTOCOL.md:599-624`. `pc` = 32 CSPRNG bytes (`config.ts:60`), `fp` = SHA-256(dPub)[0..16]. Everything after `#` — fragment, never sent to an origin. `h` from `discoverLanHost()` `pairing.ts:1255`, private-IPv4 only `pairing.ts:1274`.

**Phone** — `core/.../pairing/PairingUri.kt:53` parses; `:122-157` `parseHost` **hard-refuses** anything not an IP literal that is site-local/link-local (`HOST_NOT_PRIVATE`). `PairingClient.kt:57-63` uses a dedicated `ConnectionSpec.CLEARTEXT` OkHttp client.

**Handshake** (listener `0.0.0.0:47611`, `pairing.ts:1076`, 120 s `config.ts:62`, single use):
1. `POST /pair/kex` `{v,pid,pPub,deviceId,label,surface,appVersion,protocol}` — `PairingClient.kt:84-104` → `pairing.ts:417 handleKex`.
2. resp `{dPub,pairSalt,serverTimeMs}` `pairing.ts:489`. Phone verifies `fp` `PairingClient.kt:120-125`.
3. Both derive: `Z=X25519`, `IKM=Z‖pairingCode`, salt `"superset-companion/pair/v1"‖pairSalt` → `PRK` → `K_dev`, `K_conf_p`, `K_conf_d` (`pairing.ts:355-396`, `PROTOCOL.md:328-343`). 117-byte transcript `pairing.ts:294`.
4. `POST /pair/confirm {macPhone}` → constant-time compare `pairing.ts:665-672`; mismatch = `401 pair_bad_mac`, nothing stored.
5. Response = **sealed envelope** (kind 0x02, `K_s2c`, AAD = request-form for `POST /pair/confirm`, nonce counter **0**) carrying `macDesktop`, **`access.clientSecret` (64-hex CF service token)**, `bridge.{origin,teamDomain,aud}`, protocol range — `pairing.ts:688-740`.

**What validates the code**: nothing directly — the code is an HKDF input. A wrong `pc` ⇒ wrong PRK ⇒ `macPhone` fails. Anti-grind: 3 bad MACs burn the window `pairing.ts:841`; 5 confirm attempts/window `pairing.ts:818`; kex flood bounded separately `pairing.ts:524-567`.

**Identity storage**: desktop `keyStore.put` + device row + audit, `companion/index.ts:1393-1436`; keys under `~/.superset/companion/devices/` (`config.ts:389-406`, ACL-hardened `:433`). Phone: `SecureCompanionStore.kt:110-152` — `K_dev` and the CF secret both wrapped under the Keystore KEK; record fields at `:26-39`.

## 2. Remote transport (paired phone, away from home)

- Path: phone → `https://superset.khaira.family` (TLS, no pinning) → Cloudflare Access (service token) → cloudflared tunnel `a829d34c-…` → `127.0.0.1:47610`. `PROTOCOL.md:183-209`, `SETUP.md:36-99`.
- Auth is **two independent layers**: CF service-token headers on every request (`CompanionConstants.kt:20-21`) + per-device AES-256-GCM sealed envelope keyed by `K_dev`. Access is explicitly *not* the boundary (`PROTOCOL.md:305-312`).
- **Where the phone gets the remote URL**: *both*. It is a compile-time constant in the app — `CompanionConstants.kt:14-19` `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`/`ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN`/`ACCESS_AUD`/`ACCESS_CLIENT_ID` — and it is re-delivered in the sealed step-4 body and persisted (`SecureCompanionStore.kt:140-143`). Bridge side: `config.ts:21-37`.
- **A fresh install therefore already knows everything except the 64-hex `client_secret`.** Hostname/aud/team/client-id need no entry at all. The only cheap thing a fresh install lacks is that one secret — currently deliverable only through the LAN hop.

## 3. Pre-pairing rendezvous — none exists

- **Cloudflare Access**: one application, one policy `phone-service-token`, Service Auth only, `non_identity`, no identity policy, no allow-all (`SETUP.md:94-99`). Bridge independently validates `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` (RS256/iss/aud/exp/`common_name == c6d1295b….access`), fails **closed** on unreachable JWKS (`PROTOCOL.md:296-306`).
- Every route, including `GET /v1/ping`, calls `accessValidator.validate()` **first**: `http.ts:1150-1153` and `:1202`. Unknown paths 404 (`http.ts:1188`). So **there is no unauthenticated path through the tunnel today** — a fresh phone gets an Access HTML 403 before touching the bridge.
- **FCM cannot bootstrap**: it is desktop→phone only, and the token reaches the desktop via `POST /v1/device/register`, which is a **sealed write** requiring `K_dev` (`PROTOCOL.md:1253-1283`, `BridgeEndpoint.kt:53`). No pre-pairing FCM channel.
- **Superset's own relay does exist** and the host-service already holds a control WS to it (`packages/host-service/src/tunnel/tunnel-client-v2.ts:23,55,139` — `/v2/control`, `/v2/dial`), authenticated with the desktop's Superset account JWT. The phone has no account credential, so it is not reachable pre-pairing without new auth.

## 4. Security model of the current secret / available primitives

| Property | Value | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Length | 32 bytes / 256 bits | `config.ts:60`, `PROTOCOL.md:614` |
| Entropy | full CSPRNG, no dictionary | `pairing.ts:960` |
| Single use | window consumed on first verified MAC | `pairing.ts:678` |
| Expiry | 120 000 ms | `config.ts:62` |
| Grind budget | 3 bad MACs burn; 5 confirms/window | `pairing.ts:818,841` |

PROTOCOL constraints on pairing: `§4` goals `:556-564` (K_dev derived never transmitted; no secret rendered as text; nothing secret reaches a server; CF token bound to device during the exchange), `§4.1:566-578` LAN is a **hard requirement, no degraded path**, `§4.3:617-620` *"`pc` is 256 bits, not a human-typed short code … no PAKE is required. The code is short-lived, not short."*, `§4.6:708-728` private-IP check, `§4.7:730-739`. **A 6-digit code contradicts §4.3 explicitly — PROTOCOL.md §4 must be amended, not worked around.**

**No PAKE/SPAKE2/SRP/OPAQUE/CPace exists in either repo** (greps clean). Available reusable primitives:

- Bridge `companion/crypto.ts`: `hkdfExtract:205`, `hkdfExpandLabel:220`, `hkdfExpandInfo:238`, `hmacSha256:291`, `x25519:326` (rejects all-zero Z), `generateX25519KeyPair:353`, `seal:644` / `openSealed:600`, `constantTimeEquals:708`, `randomBytes:813`, durable-write helpers `:1232-1257`.
- Phone `core/crypto/`: `Hkdf.kt`, `KeyDerivation.kt`, `X25519.kt`, `SealedEnvelope.kt`, `ConstantTime.kt`, `Base64Url.kt`; cross-runtime differential vectors already pinned (`core/src/test/.../CrossRuntimeDifferentialVectors20260729Test.kt`).
- Missing for a real PAKE: hash-to-curve / X25519 point addition on both sides. Node exposes neither for X25519 → a true SPAKE2 needs a curve library (`@noble/curves`) on the bridge and BouncyCastle/noble-equivalent on Android. This is the single largest cost driver below.

| PROTOCOL | `§4` `:554-748` (rewrite `§4.1`, `§4.3`, `§4.6`, `§4.7`), `§2` `:270-313`, `§12` rate limits | normative amendment required |

## Viable architectures

### A. PAKE (SPAKE2 or CPace) over the tunnel hostname, behind a path-scoped Access **Bypass** policy
Add a second Cloudflare Access policy on `superset.khaira.family/v1/pair/*` with action **Bypass** (no service token). Bridge serves `/v1/pair/*` unsealed on 47610; desktop shows 6 digits; both run SPAKE2 with the digits as the password → session key → sealed step-4 delivers the CF secret exactly as today.
- **Security**: correct construction — a 6-digit password is only safe inside a PAKE, where each online guess costs one round and the window burns after 3. Offline dictionary attack is impossible by PAKE definition. Requires: strict per-source rate limit + burn on the Bypass path (that path is now *internet-reachable and unauthenticated* — it is the whole new attack surface, and it sits in the same process as the pty writer). Keep the 120 s window, single-use, and 3-strike burn from `pairing.ts:818-847`.
- **Trade-offs**: new unauthenticated internet-facing endpoint (PROTOCOL `§1.1:202-204` "loopback only" must be amended); needs a hash-to-curve-capable curve lib on **both** runtimes plus new cross-runtime differential vectors; CF policy is manual config the code can't verify.
- **Size**: large. ~600-900 LOC bridge + ~500 Kotlin + PAKE dependency on both sides + test vectors + PROTOCOL §4 rewrite. 2-3 weeks equivalent.

### B. Make the origin reachable pre-pairing, keep the existing X25519+HKDF exchange, harden with the 6 digits (no PAKE)
Ship the CF service token in the app (or a second, pairing-only Access token scoped to `/v1/pair/*`), so a fresh phone can reach the origin. Then run the **existing** kex/confirm over TLS, with the 6-digit code replacing `pc` as the HKDF input, plus a server-side burn.
- **Security**: weaker in principle — a 20-bit code without a PAKE means the confirm MAC is a verifier an attacker who reaches the endpoint can grind. Mitigated *only* by the online burn (3 strikes / 120 s / single use), which is genuinely the same posture as a bank OTP but no longer the "no dictionary to attack" property `§4.3:617` sells. Also spends goal 4 (`§4:563`): the token stops being device-bound. Note `§2.2:305-312` already says the token authenticates nothing — a stolen token yields only `unknown_device`.
- **Trade-offs**: cheapest by far, reuses `derivePairingKeys`/transcript/MAC/seal verbatim; but embeds a shared credential in a sideloaded APK, and the burn is the *only* thing between 10⁶ guesses and a device key. Consider 8-10 digits to buy 27-33 bits.
- **Size**: small. ~200 LOC bridge (new window variant + route), ~250 Kotlin (TLS pairing client + digit UI), zero new crypto, zero new test vectors. Days.

### C. Cloud rendezvous relay
Broker the two halves through a server neither has to reach directly: either a tiny Cloudflare Worker/KV keyed by the 6 digits, or Superset's existing relay (`tunnel-client-v2.ts:55,139` — desktop already holds an authenticated `/v2/control` socket). Desktop posts its ephemeral `dPub` + salt under `hash(code)`; phone fetches, posts `pPub`; sealed step-4 flows through the same slot.
- **Security**: the relay sees only public values *if* combined with a PAKE (A) — with a plain 6-digit code the relay operator or anyone who guesses the slot can MITM the kex outright. So C is a **transport** for A, not an alternative to it. Adds a third party to a design whose §4 goal 3 (`:560-562`) is "nothing secret ever reaches a server".
- **Trade-offs**: works when the tunnel itself is down or the hostname is unknown; no CF policy edit; but new deployed infrastructure to own, and the phone still needs an identity to use Superset's relay (it has none).
- **Size**: medium-large. Worker + KV + both clients ≈ A's cost plus deployment; using Superset's relay is larger still (new phone-side auth).

**Recommendation shape**: B is the honest minimum-change path if the 6-digit entropy is compensated (longer code + hard burn + audit). A is the only option that preserves the security *claims* PROTOCOL.md currently makes, and its Bypass-path exposure is the thing to review hardest. C only as a fallback transport layered on A.</result>

## Config the CF setup must fill

`~/.superset/companion/cloudflare-access-pairing.json`:
```json
{ "app_aud": "&lt;64-char lowercase hex AUD of the NEW pairing Access app&gt;",
  "client_id": "&lt;pairing service token client id, ends in .access&gt;",
  "team_domain": "khairm.cloudflareaccess.com" }
```
Start-up rejects it if `app_aud` isn't 64 hex, `client_id` doesn't end `.access`, `team_domain` differs, or either value equals the MAIN application's. The pairing token's **secret is deliberately not a field** — the desktop only validates JWTs; the secret lives in the APK. The Access app must cover `/v1/pair/*` only. Restart host-service after writing it.

## Deviations (deliberate)

- Adopted the companion agent's frozen labels (`superset-companion/pair/v2-remote`, `sc/v2-remote …`, 124-byte transcript) rather than my draft; vectors verified identical on both runtimes.
- Three new wire codes (`pair_code_wrong`, `pair_code_burned`, `pair_bad_key_agreement`); LAN codes untouched. Added a 60 s burn memo.
- Access validation runs *before* the window check (spec listed 404 first) — fail-closed on identity; difference is only observable to a valid pairing-token holder.
- Source attribution is `cf-connecting-ip`, absence refused (the socket peer behind the tunnel is always loopback).
- **I pushed back on the phone side's kex shape**, which carried the code in the request body. Cloudflare terminates the TLS, so that would have handed the edge a pairable code and voided the MITM argument. They agreed and dropped it, plus a kex-side burn rule that junk could have used to destroy a legitimate window.

No `git diff HEAD`, no untracked enumeration. Candidates derived by reading the remote-pairing surface directly via Grep/Read. Under-coverage risk on untracked files I could not list.

---

**1. [MAJOR]**
- `file`: `C:/Users/khair/Code Projects/superset-companion/core/src/main/java/family/khaira/supersetcompanion/core/pairing/PairingClient.kt`
- `line`: 223 (`handshake: RemotePairingHandshake = RemotePairingHandshake()`), with `RemotePairingHandshake.kt:80` `baseClient: OkHttpClient = OkHttpClient()`
- `summary`: Default arg re-evaluated per call → fresh OkHttpClient per remote pairing attempt instead of `CompanionCore.httpClient`.
- `failure_scenario`: `BridgeCompanionRepository.pairFromCode` (line 962) passes no handshake. User mistypes digits → `pair_code_wrong` → user-triggered retained-code retry. Each attempt allocates a new Dispatcher ExecutorService (threads idle 60 s), ConnectionPool (5 idle TLS conns, 5 min keep-alive), TaskRunner thread; nothing calls `executorService.shutdown()` / `connectionPool.evictAll()`. 3 retries + a mode switch → 4 leaked pools holding live TLS sockets to the pairing host. Every sibling (`PairingClient`, `SealedTransport` CompanionCore.kt:96, `EventStreamClient` :98) gets the shared client — this is the sole fork.
- cheaper safe alternative: thread the shared `httpClient` into the handshake (field on PairingClient, or construct the handshake once in CompanionCore). Hoist `SecureRandom()` off the per-call path.

**2. [MAJOR]**
- `file`: `C:/Users/khair/Code Projects/fork-superset-windows-arm64/.claude/worktrees/agent-a6c0cf0431904f59f/packages/host-service/src/companion/srp.ts`
- `line`: 321 (`srpSecretModPow`), called at 508 and 525; charged at `pairing.ts:1063`
- `summary`: 64-window ladder = 320 BigInt mulmods of 3072-bit operands, run synchronously on the host-service event loop, twice per kex, from unauthenticated public input.
- `failure_scenario`: Module header's own measurement ~2.8-3.3 ms/exponentiation, ~10 ms/kex on win-arm64. `MAX_KEX_COMPUTATIONS_PER_WINDOW = 64` (pairing.ts:256) with `REMOTE_PAIR_MAX_REQUESTS_PER_WINDOW = 1024` (config.ts:234) lets a flood deliver all 64 back-to-back → ~640 ms contiguous event-loop block inside the 120 s window. Same loop serves `/v1/events` WS, `/v1/answer`, push → phone answering a blocked question stalls, and the real phone's kex queues behind the flood.
- cheaper safe alternative: worker_threads pool for the two exponentiations, or at minimum `setImmediate` yields between B and S and between queued kex requests. Bounds stay as-is.

**3. [MINOR]**
- `file`: `.../packages/host-service/src/companion/srp.ts`
- `line`: 505 (`const k = srpComputeK(group)`)
- `summary`: `k = H(PAD(N) || PAD(g))` is a per-group constant, recomputed on every handshake.
- `failure_scenario`: Each kex pays two `srpPad` round-trips (`BigInt.toString(16)` → `padStart` → `Buffer.from(hex,"hex")`, srp.ts:205-219) on the 3072-bit modulus plus a SHA-256 and a hex→BigInt parse. Under flood: 64× per window, on the already-blocked loop.
- cheaper safe alternative: compute `k` (and `PAD(N)`/`PAD(g)`) in `defineGroup`, expose as readonly fields.

**4. [MINOR]**
- `file`: `.../packages/host-service/src/companion/pairing.ts`
- `line`: 1097 (also 1528, and 1808/1813 in `assertPidMatches`)
- `summary`: `pid` kept only as base64url text and re-decoded 2-3× per request, though raw `pidBytes` existed at open (line 2275) and was discarded.
- `failure_scenario`: Up to 1024 admitted requests/window each pay 2-3 base64 decodes + allocations for 16 bytes already in hand; `assertPidMatches` re-decodes the *server's own* pid on every hostile packet.
- cheaper safe alternative: store `pidBytes` on `PairingSessionCommon`; decode only the caller-supplied value.

**5. [MINOR]**
- `file`: `.../packages/host-service/src/companion/pairing.ts`
- `line`: 1356 (`evictOldestKexSessionsIfFull`)
- `summary`: Full O(n) scan on `createdAtMs` to find the oldest candidate, but the Map is append-only so insertion order already yields it — `chargePerSource` 15 lines earlier (:1341) uses the cheap `keys().next()` idiom.
- `failure_scenario`: Every introduction during a `begin` flood pays an O(17) scan plus `POSITIVE_INFINITY` bookkeeping instead of one `keys().next()`. Small absolute cost, but redundant work on the public flood path and inconsistent with the file's own eviction pattern.
- cheaper safe alternative: `state.kexSessions.keys().next()` as victim; drop the scan.

**6. [MINOR]**
- `file`: `.../apps/desktop/src/renderer/components/PairDeviceDialog/PairDeviceDialog.tsx`
- `line`: 253 (`setInterval(tick, 250)`)
- `summary`: 250 ms tick stores raw `remainingMs`, but the only consumer renders `Math.ceil(remainingMs/1000)` (line 419) → 3 of 4 renders are identical output.
- `failure_scenario`: ~480 `PairingFlow` renders per 120 s window instead of ~120. `QrCode` memoizes on `value` so the encode is skipped, but React still reconciles the ~2 800-module `&lt;path&gt;` and the whole dialog tree 4×/s, on top of the 1 s `pairingState` poll (line 325).
- cheaper safe alternative: store integer `secondsLeft` so `setState` no-ops when unchanged, or tick once per second aligned to the next boundary.
